Proof of assignment case for
21
.

Lemma 22
.

If
\mbox{{okf}}(c,f)
and
c\mathbin{/}f\hookrightarrow gcs
then

\llbracket\,\mbox{{enab}}(gcs)\,\rrbracket=\llbracket\,(\lor\,i\>:\>i\in\mbox{{labs}}(c)\>:\>\mathord{?}i)\,\rrbracket
.

Proof 5.7
.

This follows from the provable equation (nf-enab-labs) mentioned in the proof of
21
,
together with
13
.

6.
Floyd completeness

In this section we put the normal form equivalence theorem to work showing that
any IAM proof of a unary correctness judgment can be translated to one in HL+.
This sets a pattern that guides the proofs of alignment completeness. It also
gives a way to prove completeness of HL+ in the sense of Cook.

Theorem 23
.

Suppose
\mbox{{okf}}(c,f)
.
Suppose
an
is a valid annotation of
\mbox{{aut}}(c,f)
for
P\leadsto Q

and
P,Q,an
are finitely supported.
Then
c:P\leadsto Q
can be proved in HL+, using only assertions derived from
an
.

The phrase “derived from
an
” is deliberately vague,
as is the similar result of Nagasamudram and Naumann
[
NN21
]
.
As sketched by example in
section 2
,
our HL+ proof uses only a single instance of the
Do
rule and no instance of the
If
rule.
More importantly, the judgments use only assertions derived from those of
an
in simple ways.
In particular, we use boolean combinations of the following:
assertions
an(i)
, boolean expressions that occur in
c
,
and equality tests
pc=n
of the program counter variable and numeric literals;
and we use substitution instances
{an(i)}^{x}_{e}
for assignments

x:=e
that occur in
c
.
The assumption about finite support is a technicality.
It holds for assertions expressed by formulas in any usual assertion language.

Proof 6.1
.

Choose variable
pc
that is fresh with respect to
c
,
P
,
Q
, and
an
(i.e., for all
i
,
an(i)
is independent from
pc
).
Existence of such a variable is ensured by the assumption of finite support.
By
20
we have some
gcs

with
c\mathbin{/}f\hookrightarrow gcs
.
By
21
we have

\mathord{!}n;\mathsf{do}\ {gcs}\ \mathsf{od}\>\mathrel{\simeq}\>\mbox{{add}}^{pc}(c);\mathord{!}f\quad\mbox{where $n=\mbox{{lab}}(c)$.}

(13)

For a loop invariant to reason about the normal form, with an eye on
the example we might try this formula:

1\leq pc\leq f\land(\land\,i\>:\>0\leq i\leq f\>:\>pc=i\Rightarrow an(i))
.
But this only makes sense if the labels form a contiguous sequence, which
we do not require. There is no need to reason arithmetically about labels.
We define the invariant
I
as follows:

I:\quad(\lor\,i\>:\>i\in\mbox{{labs}}(c)\cup\{f\}\>:\>\mathord{?}i)\land(\land\,i\>:\>i\in\mbox{{labs}}(c)\cup\{f\}\>:\>\mathord{?}i\Rightarrow an(i))

The disjunction says the current value of
pc
is in
\mbox{{labs}}(c)\cup\{f\}
.

The next step is to obtain proofs of

b:I\land e\leadsto I\qquad\mbox{for each $e\mathrel{\shortrightarrow}b$ in $gcs$ }

(14)

To do so, first note that we have for any
m
that

\mathord{!}m:an(m)\leadsto an(m)\land\mathord{?}m

(15)

using rules
Asgn
and
Conseq
, because by freshness
pc
is not in
an(m)
.
(We are not writing explicit
\vdash
for provability of correctness judgments.)
Second, note that by definition of
I
we have valid implications

I\land\mathord{?}n\Rightarrow an(n)\qquad\mbox{and}\qquad an(n)\land\mathord{?}n\Rightarrow I\qquad\mbox{for any $n$ in $\mbox{{labs}}(c)\cup\{f\}$}

(16)

Now go by the possible cases of
b
in (
14
),
which are given by
19
.

•

b
has the form

\mathord{?}n\mathrel{\shortrightarrow}x:=e;\mathord{!}m
, where
\mbox{{sub}}(n,c)
is
x:=^{n}e

and
m=\mbox{{fsuc}}(n,c,f)
.

To show:
x:=e;\mathord{!}m:I\land\mathord{?}n\leadsto I
.
By rule
Asgn
we have

x:=e:{an(m)}^{x}_{e}\leadsto an(m)
.
By the VC in
Figure 7
we have

an(n)\Rightarrow{an(m)}^{x}_{e}
,
so using
Conseq
we get

x:=e:an(n)\leadsto an(m)
.
By fact (
15
) we have

\mathord{!}m:an(m)\leadsto an(m)\land\mathord{?}m
, so
by rule
Seq
we have
x:=e;\mathord{!}m:an(n)\leadsto an(m)\land\mathord{?}m
.
So by
Conseq
using both implications in fact (
16
) we get

x:=e;\mathord{!}m:I\land\mathord{?}n\leadsto I
.

The other cases are similar (see
[
NBN23
, Appendix D.4]
).

Having established the premises of rule
Do
, we get its conclusion:

\mathsf{do}\ {gcs}\ \mathsf{od}:I\leadsto I\land\mbox{$\lnot$}\mbox{{enab}}(gcs)

By
22
and definition of
I
,

I\land\mbox{$\lnot$}\mbox{{enab}}(gcs)
is equivalent to
I\land\mathord{?}f
,
so by consequence we get

\mathsf{do}\ {gcs}\ \mathsf{od}:I\land\mathord{?}n\leadsto I\land\mathord{?}f
.
Now
I\land\mathord{?}n
is equivalent to
an(n)\land\mathord{?}n
.
We have
P\Rightarrow an(n)
and
an(f)\Rightarrow Q

because
an
is an annotation for the spec
P\leadsto Q
.
So by consequence we get

\mathsf{do}\ {gcs}\ \mathsf{od}:P\land\mathord{?}n\leadsto Q
.
By the assignment rule and consequence using that
pc
is fresh for
P
we get

\mathord{!}n:P\leadsto P\land\mathord{?}n
,
so using the sequence rule we get

\mathord{!}n;\mathsf{do}\ {gcs}\ \mathsf{od}:P\leadsto Q

Then
Rewrite
using (
13
) yields

\mbox{{add}}^{pc}(c);\mathord{!}f:P\leadsto Q
.
By
18
and freshness of
pc
we have that

pc
is ghost in
\mbox{{add}}^{pc}(c);\mathord{!}f
. Also,
P
and
Q
are independent from
pc
.
So by rule
Ghost
we get

\mbox{{erase}}(pc,\mbox{{add}}^{pc}(c);\mathord{!}f):P\leadsto Q
.
Now using
18
together with the general law
c;\mathsf{skip}\mathrel{\simeq}c

and transitivity of
\mathrel{\simeq}
,
19
19

19

Both of which are easily
derived using the definition of
\mathrel{\simeq}
.

we have that
\mbox{{erase}}(pc,\mbox{{add}}^{pc}(c);\mathord{!}f)\mathrel{\simeq}c
,
so by
Rewrite

we get
c:P\leadsto Q
.

Corollary 24

(HL+ is Cook complete)
.

If
\models c:P\leadsto Q
and
P,Q
are finitely supported then
there is a proof of
c:P\leadsto Q
in HL+.

Proof 6.2
.

Suppose
\models c:P\leadsto Q
.
Without loss of generality assume
c
has ok labels and
\mbox{{okf}}(c,f)
for some
f
.
By completeness of IAM (
3
) there is a valid and finitely supported annotation of
\mbox{{aut}}(c,f)
for
P,Q
.
So by
23
there is a proof in HL+ of
c:P\leadsto Q
.

7.
RHL+ is alignment complete and Cook complete

In
subsection 7.1
we prove alignment completeness.
In
subsection 7.2
we prove Cook completeness and consider connections with unary logic, in particular the well known use of sequential alignment to obtain Cook completeness from a complete unary logic.

7.1.
Alignment completeness of RHL+

Our main result for
\forall\forall
properties says that
given any IAM-style proof for a program alignment automaton, one can construct an RHL+ proof.
Conditions (b) and (c) in the theorem say there is an IAM-style proof.

Theorem 25
.

Suppose we have the following.

(a)
\mbox{{okf}}(c,f)
and
\mbox{{okf}}(c^{\prime},f^{\prime})
.

(b)
an
is a valid annotation of
\prod(\mbox{{aut}}(c,f),\mbox{{aut}}(c^{\prime},f^{\prime}),L,R,J)

for
\mathcal{S}\leadsto\mathcal{T}
.

(c)
\breve{an}(i,j)\Rightarrow L\lor R\lor J\lor[\mathit{fin}|\mathit{fin}^{\prime}]
for all control points
(i,j)
of

\prod(\mbox{{aut}}(c,f),\mbox{{aut}}(c^{\prime},f^{\prime}),L,R,J)
.

(d)
an
,
\mathcal{S}
,
\mathcal{T}
,
L
,
R
, and
J
all have finite support.

Then the judgment
c\mathbin{\mid}c^{\prime}:\mathcal{S}\mathrel{\mbox{\footnotesize$\raisebox{-0.24113pt}{$\thickapprox$}\hskip-4.13332pt>$}}\mathcal{T}
has a proof in RHL+.

The proof of the Theorem yields a deductive proof that uses only relational assertions derived in simple ways from the relations
an(i,j)
of the annotation together with
L
,
R
, and
J
.
Specifically, the proof uses boolean combinations
of the annotation’s assertions, conjunctions with conditional tests in the code (and with
L
and
R
),
and substitutions for expressions in assignment commands.

Restriction (a) in
25
is just a technicality.
The
okf
condition says labels of
c
are unique and do not include
f
.
Labels have no effect on program semantics so they can always be chosen to satisfy the condition. Restriction (d) certainly holds when specs are given by formulas in some assertion language;
it is a technicality to ensure that a fresh
pc
variable can be chosen for application of
21
.

Proof 7.1
.

Suppose
\mathcal{S},\mathcal{T},c,c^{\prime},f,f^{\prime},L,R,J
and
an
satisfy the hypotheses (a)–(d) of the theorem.
Choose variable
pc
that is fresh with respect to
\mathcal{S},\mathcal{T},c,c^{\prime},an,L,R,J
.
To be precise:
\mbox{{indep}}(pc|pc,\mathcal{S})
,
\mbox{{indep}}(pc|pc,\mathcal{T})
,
pc
does not occur in
c
or
c^{\prime}
,
and
L
,
R
,
J
are independent from
pc
on both sides,
as is
an(i,j)
for all
i,j
.
Existence of such a variable is ensured by hypothesis (d) of finite support.

By Lemma
20
there are
gcs
and
gcs^{\prime}
such that

c\mathbin{/}f\hookrightarrow gcs
and
c^{\prime}\mathbin{/}f^{\prime}\hookrightarrow gcs^{\prime}
.
Let
n=\mbox{{lab}}(c)
and
n^{\prime}=\mbox{{lab}}(c^{\prime})
.
By Theorem
21
we have

\begin{array}[]{l}\mathord{!}n;\mathsf{do}\ {gcs}\ \mathsf{od}\>\mathrel{\simeq}\>\mbox{{add}}^{pc}(c);\mathord{!}f\\
\mathord{!}n^{\prime};\mathsf{do}\ {gcs^{\prime}}\ \mathsf{od}\>\mathrel{\simeq}\>\mbox{{add}}^{pc}(c^{\prime});\mathord{!}f^{\prime}\end{array}

(17)

Define store relation
\mathcal{Q}
to be
\mathcal{Q}_{an}\land\mathcal{Q}_{pc}
where

\begin{array}[]{l}\mathcal{Q}_{an}:\qquad(\land\,i,j\>:\>i\in\mbox{{labs}}(c)\cup\{f\}\land j\in\mbox{{labs}}(c^{\prime})\cup\{f^{\prime}\}\>:\>\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.1pt[$}\mathord{?}i\mathbin{\mid}\mathord{?}j\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$]\hskip-2.1pt\rangle$ }\Rightarrow an(i,j))\\
\mathcal{Q}_{pc}:\qquad(\lor\,i,j\>:\>i\in\mbox{{labs}}(c)\cup\{f\}\land j\in\mbox{{labs}}(c^{\prime})\cup\{f^{\prime}\}\>:\>\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.1pt[$}\mathord{?}i\mathbin{\mid}\mathord{?}j\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$]\hskip-2.1pt\rangle$ })\end{array}

We will derive

\mathsf{do}\ {gcs}\ \mathsf{od}\mathbin{\mid}\mathsf{do}\ {gcs^{\prime}}\ \mathsf{od}:\mathcal{Q}\mathrel{\mbox{\footnotesize$\raisebox{-0.24113pt}{$\thickapprox$}\hskip-4.13332pt>$}}\mathcal{Q}\land\mbox{$\lnot$}\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.0pt[$}\mbox{{enab}}(gcs)\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.1pt]$}\land\mbox{$\lnot$}\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$[\hskip-2.1pt\rangle$}\mbox{{enab}}(gcs^{\prime})\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$]\hskip-2.0pt\rangle$}

(18)

using rule
rDo
instantiated with
\mathcal{Q}:=\mathcal{Q}
,
\mathcal{L}:=\tilde{L}
, and
\mathcal{R}:=\tilde{R}
.
The side condition of
rDo
is

\mathcal{Q}\Rightarrow(\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.0pt[$}\mbox{{enab}}(gcs)\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.1pt]$}=\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$[\hskip-2.1pt\rangle$}\mbox{{enab}}(gcs^{\prime})\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$]\hskip-2.0pt\rangle$})\lor(\tilde{L}\land\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.0pt[$}\mbox{{enab}}(gcs)\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.1pt]$})\lor(\tilde{R}\land\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$[\hskip-2.1pt\rangle$}\mbox{{enab}}(gcs^{\prime})\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$]\hskip-2.0pt\rangle$})

(19)

To prove (
19
), first rewrite
\mathcal{Q}
using distributivity and renaming dummies, to the equivalent form

(\lor\,i,i^{\prime}\>:\>\>:\>\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.1pt[$}\mathord{?}i\mathbin{\mid}\mathord{?}i^{\prime}\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$]\hskip-2.1pt\rangle$ }\land(\land\,k,k^{\prime}\>:\>\>:\>(\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.1pt[$}\mathord{?}k\mathbin{\mid}\mathord{?}k^{\prime}\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$]\hskip-2.1pt\rangle$ }\Rightarrow an(k,k^{\prime}))))

where we omit that
i,k
range over
\mbox{{labs}}(c)\cup\{f\}

and
i^{\prime},k^{\prime}
range over
\mbox{{labs}}(c^{\prime})\cup\{f^{\prime}\}
.
This implies

(\lor\,i,i^{\prime}\>:\>\>:\>\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.1pt[$}\mathord{?}i\mathbin{\mid}\mathord{?}i^{\prime}\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$]\hskip-2.1pt\rangle$ }\land an(i,i^{\prime}))

Thus by hypothesis (c), any
\mathcal{Q}
-state satisfies

\tilde{L}\lor\tilde{R}\lor\tilde{J}\lor\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.1pt[$}\mathord{?}f\mathbin{\mid}\mathord{?}f^{\prime}\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$]\hskip-2.1pt\rangle$ }
.
We show each of these disjuncts implies the right side of (
19
).

•

\tilde{L}
implies
\tilde{L}\land\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.0pt[$}\mbox{{enab}}(gcs)\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.1pt]$}

because (i)
L
is a set of states of the alignment automaton,
with control on the left ranging over
\mbox{{labs}}(c)\cup\{f\}
,
(ii) by liveness (Def.
4.1
),
L
allows transitions
by
\mbox{{aut}}(c,f)
, and so excludes control being at
f
,
and (iii)
\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.0pt[$}\mbox{{enab}}(gcs)\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.1pt]$}
means control on the left is in
\mbox{{labs}}(c)
,
by Lemma
22
.

•

\tilde{R}
implies
\tilde{R}\land\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$[\hskip-2.1pt\rangle$}\mbox{{enab}}(gcs^{\prime})\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$]\hskip-2.0pt\rangle$}

for reasons symmetric to the
L
case

•

\tilde{J}
implies
\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.0pt[$}\mbox{{enab}}(gcs)\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.1pt]$}
and
\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$[\hskip-2.1pt\rangle$}\mbox{{enab}}(gcs^{\prime})\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$]\hskip-2.0pt\rangle$}
are both true
(using liveness and Lemma
22
again),
so
\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.0pt[$}\mbox{{enab}}(gcs)\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.1pt]$}=\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$[\hskip-2.1pt\rangle$}\mbox{{enab}}(gcs^{\prime})\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$]\hskip-2.0pt\rangle$}

•

\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.1pt[$}\mathord{?}f\mathbin{\mid}\mathord{?}f^{\prime}\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$]\hskip-2.1pt\rangle$ }
implies
both
\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.0pt[$}\mbox{{enab}}(gcs)\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.1pt]$}
and
\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$[\hskip-2.1pt\rangle$}\mbox{{enab}}(gcs^{\prime})\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$]\hskip-2.0pt\rangle$}
are false (again using Lemma
22
)
so
\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.0pt[$}\mbox{{enab}}(gcs)\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.1pt]$}=\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$[\hskip-2.1pt\rangle$}\mbox{{enab}}(gcs^{\prime})\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$]\hskip-2.0pt\rangle$}

So the side condition (
19
) of
rDo
is proved.
Before proceeding to prove the premises for
rDo
, note that we can prove

\mathord{!}m\mathbin{\mid}\mathord{!}m^{\prime}:\mathcal{P}\mathrel{\mbox{\footnotesize$\raisebox{-0.24113pt}{$\thickapprox$}\hskip-4.13332pt>$}}\mathcal{P}\land\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.0pt[$}\mathord{?}m\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.1pt]$}\land\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$[\hskip-2.1pt\rangle$}\mathord{?}m^{\prime}\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$]\hskip-2.0pt\rangle$}

(20)

for any
\mathcal{P}
in which
pc
does not occur, and any
m,m^{\prime}
,
using rules
rAsgn
and
rConseq
.
Also, by definition of
\mathcal{Q}
we have valid implications

\begin{array}[t]{l}\mathcal{Q}\land\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.1pt[$}\mathord{?}m\mathbin{\mid}\mathord{?}m^{\prime}\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$]\hskip-2.1pt\rangle$ }\Rightarrow an(m,m^{\prime})\qquad\mbox{and}\qquad an(m,m^{\prime})\land\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.1pt[$}\mathord{?}m\mathbin{\mid}\mathord{?}m^{\prime}\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$]\hskip-2.1pt\rangle$ }\Rightarrow\mathcal{Q}\\
\mbox{for any $m$ in $\mbox{{labs}}(c)\cup\{f\}$ and
$m^{\prime}$ in $\mbox{{labs}}(c^{\prime})\cup\{f^{\prime}\}$}\end{array}

(21)

From condition (c) of the theorem we get

an(i,j)\land\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.1pt[$}\mathord{?}i\mathbin{\mid}\mathord{?}j\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$]\hskip-2.1pt\rangle$ }\Rightarrow\tilde{L}\lor\tilde{R}\lor\tilde{J}\lor\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.1pt[$}\mathord{?}\mathit{fin}\mathbin{\mid}\mathord{?}\mathit{fin}^{\prime}\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$]\hskip-2.1pt\rangle$ }

for all
i,j
(by definitions), and hence

an(i,j)\land\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.1pt[$}\mathord{?}i\mathbin{\mid}\mathord{?}j\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$]\hskip-2.1pt\rangle$ }\land\mbox{$\lnot$}\tilde{L}\land\mbox{$\lnot$}\tilde{R}\Rightarrow\tilde{J}\quad\mbox{for all $i,j$ with $i\neq\mathit{fin}$ or $j\neq\mathit{fin}^{\prime}$}

(22)

There are three sets of premises of
rDo
for the loops in (
18
), with these forms:

(left-only)

b\mathbin{\mid}\mathsf{skip}:\mathcal{Q}\land\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.0pt[$}e\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.1pt]$}\land\tilde{L}\mathrel{\mbox{\footnotesize$\raisebox{-0.24113pt}{$\thickapprox$}\hskip-4.13332pt>$}}\mathcal{Q}

for each
e\mathrel{\shortrightarrow}b
in
gcs

(right-only)

\mathsf{skip}\mathbin{\mid}b^{\prime}:\mathcal{Q}\land\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$[\hskip-2.1pt\rangle$}e^{\prime}\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$]\hskip-2.0pt\rangle$}\land\tilde{R}\mathrel{\mbox{\footnotesize$\raisebox{-0.24113pt}{$\thickapprox$}\hskip-4.13332pt>$}}\mathcal{Q}

for each
e^{\prime}\mathrel{\shortrightarrow}b^{\prime}
in
gcs^{\prime}

(joint)

b\mathbin{\mid}b^{\prime}:\mathcal{Q}\land\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.1pt[$}e\mathbin{\mid}e^{\prime}\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$]\hskip-2.1pt\rangle$ }\land\mbox{$\lnot$}\tilde{L}\land\mbox{$\lnot$}\tilde{R}\mathrel{\mbox{\footnotesize$\raisebox{-0.24113pt}{$\thickapprox$}\hskip-4.13332pt>$}}\mathcal{Q}

for each
e\mathrel{\shortrightarrow}b
in
gcs
and
e^{\prime}\mathrel{\shortrightarrow}b^{\prime}
in
gcs^{\prime}

By Lemma
19
, the guarded commands in
gcs
and
gcs^{\prime}
have six possible forms,
so there are six left-only cases to consider, six right-only, and 36 joint ones.
We start with the latter.

Joint cases

For each of the six possibilities for
e\mathrel{\shortrightarrow}b
in
gcs
for
c
,
we must consider it with each of the six possibilities for
e^{\prime}\mathrel{\shortrightarrow}b^{\prime}
in
gcs^{\prime}
for
c^{\prime}
.
We give the argument for one case, with skip on both sides.

•

\mathord{!}m\mathbin{\mid}\mathord{!}m^{\prime}:\;\mathcal{Q}\land\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.1pt[$}\mathord{?}k\mathbin{\mid}\mathord{?}k^{\prime}\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$]\hskip-2.1pt\rangle$ }\land\mbox{$\lnot$}\tilde{L}\land\mbox{$\lnot$}\tilde{R}\mathrel{\mbox{\footnotesize$\raisebox{-0.24113pt}{$\thickapprox$}\hskip-4.13332pt>$}}\mathcal{Q}
, where

\mbox{{sub}}(k,c)=\mathsf{skip}^{k}
,
m=\mbox{{fsuc}}(k,c,f)
,

\mbox{{sub}}(k^{\prime},c^{\prime})=\mathsf{skip}^{k^{\prime}}
,
m=\mbox{{fsuc}}(k^{\prime},c^{\prime},f^{\prime})
.

By (
20
) (and freshness of
pc
) we have

\mathord{!}m\mathbin{\mid}\mathord{!}m^{\prime}:an(m,m^{\prime})\mathrel{\mbox{\footnotesize$\raisebox{-0.24113pt}{$\thickapprox$}\hskip-4.13332pt>$}}an(m,m^{\prime})\land\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.1pt[$}\mathord{?}m\mathbin{\mid}\mathord{?}m^{\prime}\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$]\hskip-2.1pt\rangle$ }
.

So by
rConseq
using the second implication in (
21
) we have

\mathord{!}m\mathbin{\mid}\mathord{!}m^{\prime}:an(m,m^{\prime})\mathrel{\mbox{\footnotesize$\raisebox{-0.24113pt}{$\thickapprox$}\hskip-4.13332pt>$}}\mathcal{Q}

(23)

By the first implication in (
21
) we have

\mathcal{Q}\land\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.1pt[$}\mathord{?}k\mathbin{\mid}\mathord{?}k^{\prime}\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$]\hskip-2.1pt\rangle$ }\Rightarrow an(k,k^{\prime})
.
So using (
22
) we get

\mathcal{Q}\land\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.1pt[$}\mathord{?}k\mathbin{\mid}\mathord{?}k^{\prime}\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$]\hskip-2.1pt\rangle$ }\land\mbox{$\lnot$}\tilde{L}\land\mbox{$\lnot$}\tilde{R}\Rightarrow\tilde{J}
.
By validity of the annotation, we have the VC in the first row of
Figure 9
,
i.e.,
J\land\breve{an}(k,k^{\prime})\Rightarrow\hat{an}(m,m^{\prime})
.
Then by Lemma
12
we get the
pc
-encoded form

\tilde{J}\land\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.1pt[$}\mathord{?}k\mathbin{\mid}\mathord{?}k^{\prime}\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$]\hskip-2.1pt\rangle$ }\land an(k,k^{\prime})\Rightarrow an(m,m^{\prime})
.
So this is valid:

\mathcal{Q}\land\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.1pt[$}\mathord{?}k\mathbin{\mid}\mathord{?}k^{\prime}\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$]\hskip-2.1pt\rangle$ }\land\mbox{$\lnot$}\tilde{L}\land\mbox{$\lnot$}\tilde{R}\Rightarrow an(m,m^{\prime})

Using this with
rConseq
and (
23
) yields

\mathord{!}m\mathbin{\mid}\mathord{!}m^{\prime}:\;\mathcal{Q}\land\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.1pt[$}\mathord{?}k\mathbin{\mid}\mathord{?}k^{\prime}\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$]\hskip-2.1pt\rangle$ }\land\mbox{$\lnot$}\tilde{L}\land\mbox{$\lnot$}\tilde{R}\mathrel{\mbox{\footnotesize$\raisebox{-0.24113pt}{$\thickapprox$}\hskip-4.13332pt>$}}\mathcal{Q}
.

We refrain from spelling out details of the remaining joint cases (more can be found in
[
NBN23
]
).
The arguments are all similar: every case uses a VC and rule
rConseq
,
together with (
20
).
Cases that involve an assignment in the original program
c
or
c^{\prime}
also
use rules
rAsgnAsgn
,
rSkipAsgn
, or
rAsgnSkip
.
Cases that involve havoc use the corresponding rules.

Left-only cases.

These cases are proved using the same rules as the joint cases,
plus one additional rule:
rDisj
, in the form
rDisjN
derived from it (see
Figure 3
).
This is needed due to the following complication.
The joint cases determine a starting pair and ending pair of control points,
which determines which VC to appeal to.
The left-only cases do not determine a control point on the right side; instead
we have VCs for each possible point on the right (
Figure 10
).
So we go by cases on the possible control points on the right, for which purpose
we make the following observation.
In virtue of the conjunct
\mathcal{Q}_{pc}
of
\mathcal{Q}
, we have that
\mathcal{Q}
is equivalent to this
disjunction over control points:

(\lor\,i,j\>:\>i\in\mbox{{labs}}(c)\cup\{f\}\land j\in\mbox{{labs}}(c^{\prime})\cup\{f^{\prime}\}\>:\>\mathcal{Q}^{i,j})

where
\mathcal{Q}^{i,j}
is defined to say control is at those points:

\hbox{\pagecolor{light-gray}$\mathcal{Q}^{i,j}$}\mathrel{\,\hat{=}\,}\mathcal{Q}\land\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.1pt[$}\mathord{?}i\mathbin{\mid}\mathord{?}j\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$]\hskip-2.1pt\rangle$ }
.
Now we have the equivalence

\mathcal{Q}\land\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.0pt[$}\mathord{?}i\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.1pt]$}\Leftrightarrow(\lor\,j\>:\>j\in\mbox{{labs}}(c^{\prime})\cup\{f^{\prime}\}\>:\>\mathcal{Q}^{i,j})\quad\mbox{for any $i$}

With this we can proceed to prove the left-only premises.
We give only the assignment case.

•

x:=e;\mathord{!}m\mathbin{\mid}\mathsf{skip}:\mathcal{Q}\land\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.0pt[$}\mathord{?}k\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.1pt]$}\land\tilde{L}\mathrel{\mbox{\footnotesize$\raisebox{-0.24113pt}{$\thickapprox$}\hskip-4.13332pt>$}}\mathcal{Q}
, where

\mbox{{sub}}(k,c)=x:=^{k}e
and
m=\mbox{{fsuc}}(k,c,f)
.

In accord with the discussion above, we have that

\mathcal{Q}\land\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.0pt[$}\mathord{?}k\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.1pt]$}
is equivalent to

(\lor\,j\>:\>\>:\>\mathcal{Q}^{k,j})
(omitting the range
j\in\mbox{{labs}}(c^{\prime})\cup\{f^{\prime}\}
),
so the goal can be obtained by
rConseq

from

x:=e;\mathord{!}m\mathbin{\mid}\mathsf{skip}:(\lor\,j\>:\>\>:\>\mathcal{Q}^{k,j})\land\tilde{L}\mathrel{\mbox{\footnotesize$\raisebox{-0.24113pt}{$\thickapprox$}\hskip-4.13332pt>$}}\mathcal{Q}

In turn, this can be obtained by derived rule
rDisjN
from judgments

x:=e;\mathord{!}m\mathbin{\mid}\mathsf{skip}:\mathcal{Q}^{k,j}\land\tilde{L}\mathrel{\mbox{\footnotesize$\raisebox{-0.24113pt}{$\thickapprox$}\hskip-4.13332pt>$}}\mathcal{Q}\quad\mbox{for every $j$.}

(24)

for all
j
(in range
j\in\mbox{{labs}}(c^{\prime})\cup\{f^{\prime}\}
that we continue to omit).
It remains to prove (
24
) for arbitrary
j
.

By
rAsgnSkip
and
rConseq
(using that
\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$[\hskip-2.1pt\rangle$}\mathord{?}j\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$]\hskip-2.0pt\rangle$}
is independent from
pc
on the left) we get

\mathord{!}m\mathbin{\mid}\mathsf{skip}:an(m,j)\land\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$[\hskip-2.1pt\rangle$}\mathord{?}j\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$]\hskip-2.0pt\rangle$}\mathrel{\mbox{\footnotesize$\raisebox{-0.24113pt}{$\thickapprox$}\hskip-4.13332pt>$}}an(m,j)\land\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.1pt[$}\mathord{?}m\mathbin{\mid}\mathord{?}j\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$]\hskip-2.1pt\rangle$ }

from which using (
21
) we get

\mathord{!}m\mathbin{\mid}\mathsf{skip}:an(m,j)\land\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$[\hskip-2.1pt\rangle$}\mathord{?}j\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$]\hskip-2.0pt\rangle$}\mathrel{\mbox{\footnotesize$\raisebox{-0.24113pt}{$\thickapprox$}\hskip-4.13332pt>$}}\mathcal{Q}

By
rAsgnSkip
, using that
\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$[\hskip-2.1pt\rangle$}\mathord{?}j\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$]\hskip-2.0pt\rangle$}
is independent from
x
because
pc
is fresh, we can prove

x:=e\mathbin{\mid}\mathsf{skip}:{an(m,j)}^{x|}_{e|}\land\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$[\hskip-2.1pt\rangle$}\mathord{?}j\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$]\hskip-2.0pt\rangle$}\mathrel{\mbox{\footnotesize$\raisebox{-0.24113pt}{$\thickapprox$}\hskip-4.13332pt>$}}an(m,j)\land\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$[\hskip-2.1pt\rangle$}\mathord{?}j\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$]\hskip-2.0pt\rangle$}

By derived rule
rSeqSkip
(
Figure 3
),
from the above we get

x:=e;\mathord{!}m\mathbin{\mid}\mathsf{skip}:{an(m,j)}^{x|}_{e|}\land\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$[\hskip-2.1pt\rangle$}\mathord{?}j\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$]\hskip-2.0pt\rangle$}\mathrel{\mbox{\footnotesize$\raisebox{-0.24113pt}{$\thickapprox$}\hskip-4.13332pt>$}}\mathcal{Q}

The disjunction over
j
was introduced so that we can appeal to a VC,
specifically the lifted VC for
((k,j),(m,j))
.
It is an instance of the second line in
Figure 10
and it says this is valid:

\tilde{L}\land\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.1pt[$}\mathord{?}k\mathbin{\mid}\mathord{?}j\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$]\hskip-2.1pt\rangle$ }\land an(k,j)\Rightarrow{an(m,j)}^{x\mathbin{\mid}}_{e\mathbin{\mid}}
,
so by
rConseq
we get

x:=e;\mathord{!}m\mathbin{\mid}\mathsf{skip}:\tilde{L}\land\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.1pt[$}\mathord{?}k\mathbin{\mid}\mathord{?}j\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$]\hskip-2.1pt\rangle$ }\land an(k,j)\mathrel{\mbox{\footnotesize$\raisebox{-0.24113pt}{$\thickapprox$}\hskip-4.13332pt>$}}\mathcal{Q}

By definitions we have
\mathcal{Q}^{k,j}\land\tilde{L}\Rightarrow\tilde{L}\land\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.1pt[$}\mathord{?}k\mathbin{\mid}\mathord{?}j\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$]\hskip-2.1pt\rangle$ }\land an(k,j)
.
Using this with
rConseq
yields (
24
) and we are done with this case.

The other left-only cases are similar.
The right-only cases are symmetric with the left-only cases.
We omit them all and proceed.

Finishing the proof.

Having proved the premises and the side condition (
19
), rule
rDo
yields
(
18
). The remaining steps are similar to corresponding steps in the proof of
the Floyd completeness Theorem
23
and we spell them out.

Using
22
twice, and the definition of
\mathcal{Q}
,
we have

\mathcal{Q}\land\mbox{$\lnot$}\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.0pt[$}\mbox{{enab}}(gcs)\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.1pt]$}\land\mbox{$\lnot$}\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$[\hskip-2.1pt\rangle$}\mbox{{enab}}(gcs^{\prime})\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$]\hskip-2.0pt\rangle$}\Rightarrow\mathcal{Q}\land\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.1pt[$}\mathord{?}f\mathbin{\mid}\mathord{?}f^{\prime}\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$]\hskip-2.1pt\rangle$ }

So, using the first implication in (
21
)
and assumption (b) of the theorem (which says
an(f,f^{\prime})\Rightarrow\mathcal{T}
since
an

is an annotation for
\mathcal{S}\mathrel{\mbox{\footnotesize$\raisebox{-0.24113pt}{$\thickapprox$}\hskip-4.13332pt>$}}\mathcal{T}
),
we can use

rConseq
with (
18
) to get

\mathsf{do}\ {gcs}\ \mathsf{od}\mathbin{\mid}\mathsf{do}\ {gcs^{\prime}}\ \mathsf{od}:\mathcal{Q}\mathrel{\mbox{\footnotesize$\raisebox{-0.24113pt}{$\thickapprox$}\hskip-4.13332pt>$}}\mathcal{T}

Using the second implication in (
21
)
and assumption (b) (which says
\mathcal{S}\Rightarrow an(n,n^{\prime})

since
n,n^{\prime}
are the initial control points),
we have
\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.1pt[$}\mathord{?}n\mathbin{\mid}\mathord{?}n^{\prime}\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$]\hskip-2.1pt\rangle$ }\land\mathcal{S}\Rightarrow\mathcal{Q}
,
so by
rConseq
we get

\mathsf{do}\ {gcs}\ \mathsf{od}\mathbin{\mid}\mathsf{do}\ {gcs^{\prime}}\ \mathsf{od}:\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.1pt[$}\mathord{?}n\mathbin{\mid}\mathord{?}n^{\prime}\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$]\hskip-2.1pt\rangle$ }\land\mathcal{S}\mathrel{\mbox{\footnotesize$\raisebox{-0.24113pt}{$\thickapprox$}\hskip-4.13332pt>$}}\mathcal{T}

By freshness assumption for
pc
, by (
20
)
we have a proof of
\mathord{!}n\mathbin{\mid}\mathord{!}n^{\prime}:\mathcal{S}\mathrel{\mbox{\footnotesize$\raisebox{-0.24113pt}{$\thickapprox$}\hskip-4.13332pt>$}}\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.1pt[$}\mathord{?}n\mathbin{\mid}\mathord{?}n^{\prime}\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$]\hskip-2.1pt\rangle$ }\land\mathcal{S}
.
So by
rSeq
we get

\mathord{!}n;\mathsf{do}\ {gcs}\ \mathsf{od}\mathbin{\mid}\mathord{!}n^{\prime};\mathsf{do}\ {gcs^{\prime}}\ \mathsf{od}:\mathcal{S}\mathrel{\mbox{\footnotesize$\raisebox{-0.24113pt}{$\thickapprox$}\hskip-4.13332pt>$}}\mathcal{T}

Now using rule
rRewrite
with the
equivalences (
17
) we get

\mbox{{add}}^{pc}(c);\mathord{!}f\mathbin{\mid}\mbox{{add}}^{pc}(c^{\prime});\mathord{!}f^{\prime}:\mathcal{S}\mathrel{\mbox{\footnotesize$\raisebox{-0.24113pt}{$\thickapprox$}\hskip-4.13332pt>$}}\mathcal{T}

By freshness of
pc
and Lemma
18
,
it has the ghost property for both

\mbox{{add}}^{pc}(c);\mathord{!}f
and
\mbox{{add}}^{pc}(c^{\prime});\mathord{!}f^{\prime}
,
and does not occur in
\mathcal{S}
or
\mathcal{T}
, so by rule
rGhost
we get

\mbox{{erase}}(pc,\mbox{{add}}^{pc}(c);\mathord{!}f)\mathbin{\mid}\mbox{{erase}}(\mbox{{add}}^{pc}(c^{\prime});\mathord{!}f^{\prime}):\mathcal{S}\mathrel{\mbox{\footnotesize$\raisebox{-0.24113pt}{$\thickapprox$}\hskip-4.13332pt>$}}\mathcal{T}

(25)

By definition of
erase
, we have

\mbox{{erase}}(pc,\mbox{{add}}^{pc}(c);\mathord{!}f)=\mbox{{erase}}(pc,\mbox{{add}}^{pc}(c));\mathsf{skip}

and also

\mbox{{erase}}(\mbox{{add}}^{pc}(c^{\prime});\mathord{!}f^{\prime})=\mbox{{erase}}(pc,\mbox{{add}}^{pc}(c^{\prime}));\mathsf{skip}
.
So using Lemma
18
together with the general law
c;\mathsf{skip}\mathrel{\simeq}c

and transitivity of
\mathrel{\simeq}
, we have

\mbox{{erase}}(pc,\mbox{{add}}^{pc}(c);\mathord{!}f)\mathrel{\simeq}c\qquad\mbox{and}\qquad\mbox{{erase}}(pc,\mbox{{add}}^{pc}(c^{\prime});\mathord{!}f^{\prime})\mathrel{\simeq}c^{\prime}

Using these equivalences with
rRewrite
, from (
25
) we obtain

c\mathbin{\mid}c^{\prime}:\mathcal{S}\mathrel{\mbox{\footnotesize$\raisebox{-0.24113pt}{$\thickapprox$}\hskip-4.13332pt>$}}\mathcal{T}
.

7.2.
Cook completeness revisited

Cook completeness can be proved as a consequence of alignment completeness.

Theorem 26

(Cook completeness of RHL+)
.

Suppose
\models c\mathbin{\mid}c^{\prime}:\mathcal{S}\mathrel{\mbox{\footnotesize$\raisebox{-0.24113pt}{$\thickapprox$}\hskip-4.13332pt>$}}\mathcal{T}
and
\mathcal{S},\mathcal{T}
are finitely supported.
Then there is a proof of
c\mathbin{\mid}c^{\prime}:\mathcal{S}\mathrel{\mbox{\footnotesize$\raisebox{-0.24113pt}{$\thickapprox$}\hskip-4.13332pt>$}}\mathcal{T}
in RHL+.

Proof 7.2
.

Suppose
\models c\mathbin{\mid}c^{\prime}:\mathcal{S}\mathrel{\mbox{\footnotesize$\raisebox{-0.24113pt}{$\thickapprox$}\hskip-4.13332pt>$}}\mathcal{T}
, and assume wlog
that
\mbox{{okf}}(c,f)
and
\mbox{{okf}}(c^{\prime},f^{\prime})
.
Using Lemma
8

we have
\mbox{{aut}}(c,f),\mbox{{aut}}(c^{\prime},f^{\prime})\models\mathcal{S}\mathrel{\mbox{\footnotesize$\raisebox{-0.24113pt}{$\thickapprox$}\hskip-4.13332pt>$}}\mathcal{T}
.
By
5
there are
L,R,J,an
such that

an
is a valid annotation of
\prod(\mbox{{aut}}(c,f),\mbox{{aut}}(c^{\prime},f^{\prime}),L,R,J)
for
\mathcal{S}\leadsto\mathcal{T}
and for any
i,j
we have that

\breve{an}(i,j)\Rightarrow L\lor R\lor J\lor[\mathit{fin}|\mathit{fin}^{\prime}]

which is condition (c) of Theorem
25
.
Moreover these are finitely supported as required by condition (d).
We have conditions (a) and (b) as well, so by the theorem we
get a proof of

c\mathbin{\mid}c^{\prime}:\mathcal{S}\mathrel{\mbox{\footnotesize$\raisebox{-0.24113pt}{$\thickapprox$}\hskip-4.13332pt>$}}\mathcal{T}
in RHL+.

Prior Cook completeness results for RHLs were based on a left-first sequential alignment rule like our
rLRseq
in
Figure 3
,
together with unary HL and a way to represent or interpret the one-sided judgments

c\mathbin{\mid}\mathsf{skip}:\mathcal{P}\mathrel{\mbox{\footnotesize$\raisebox{-0.24113pt}{$\thickapprox$}\hskip-4.13332pt>$}}\mathcal{Q}
and

\mathsf{skip}\mathbin{\mid}c^{\prime}:\mathcal{Q}\mathrel{\mbox{\footnotesize$\raisebox{-0.24113pt}{$\thickapprox$}\hskip-4.13332pt>$}}\mathcal{R}
as unary judgments in HL.
It is instructive to consider that approach in our setting.
But first we indulge in little detour.

Our formulation of RHL+ does not include unary judgments but it subsumes HL+ in the following sense.
A unary judgment
c:P\leadsto Q
can be represented by
the relational judgment
c\mathbin{\mid}\mathsf{skip}:\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.0pt[$}P\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.1pt]$}\mathrel{\mbox{\footnotesize$\raisebox{-0.24113pt}{$\thickapprox$}\hskip-4.13332pt>$}}\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.0pt[$}Q\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.1pt]$}
,
as well as by
\mathsf{skip}\mathbin{\mid}c:\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$[\hskip-2.1pt\rangle$}P\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$]\hskip-2.0pt\rangle$}\mathrel{\mbox{\footnotesize$\raisebox{-0.24113pt}{$\thickapprox$}\hskip-4.13332pt>$}}\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$[\hskip-2.1pt\rangle$}Q\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$]\hskip-2.0pt\rangle$}
.
That is, the judgments express the same semantic property of
c
.
So if the judgment
c:P\leadsto Q
is valid then we have

\models c\mathbin{\mid}\mathsf{skip}:\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.0pt[$}P\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.1pt]$}\mathrel{\mbox{\footnotesize$\raisebox{-0.24113pt}{$\thickapprox$}\hskip-4.13332pt>$}}\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.0pt[$}Q\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.1pt]$}
,
hence by
26

one can prove
c\mathbin{\mid}\mathsf{skip}:\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.0pt[$}P\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.1pt]$}\mathrel{\mbox{\footnotesize$\raisebox{-0.24113pt}{$\thickapprox$}\hskip-4.13332pt>$}}\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.0pt[$}Q\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.1pt]$}
in RHL+.
Furthermore, any proof in HL+ gives rise to a proof in RHL+ with the same structure and intermediate assertions. This is because every rule in HL+, when translated to the representation
c\mathbin{\mid}\mathsf{skip}:\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.0pt[$}P\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.1pt]$}\mathrel{\mbox{\footnotesize$\raisebox{-0.24113pt}{$\thickapprox$}\hskip-4.13332pt>$}}\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.0pt[$}Q\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.1pt]$}
, is a derivable rule in RHL+.
Indeed, they are instances of the more general one-sided rules
rAsgnSkip
,

rIfSkip
, etc. in
Figure 2
and
Figure 3
.

Having completed the detour we return to the reduction of relational reasoning to unary.
To this end we need to interpret judgments of the forms

c\mathbin{\mid}\mathsf{skip}:\mathcal{P}\mathrel{\mbox{\footnotesize$\raisebox{-0.24113pt}{$\thickapprox$}\hskip-4.13332pt>$}}\mathcal{Q}
and

\mathsf{skip}\mathbin{\mid}c^{\prime}:\mathcal{Q}\mathrel{\mbox{\footnotesize$\raisebox{-0.24113pt}{$\thickapprox$}\hskip-4.13332pt>$}}\mathcal{R}
—which are one-sided in terms of the code, but which involve general relational formulas— as unary judgments.
Such an interpretation is easier to formulate in terms of syntactic formulas.
By renaming the variables of
c
and
c^{\prime}
to be disjoint, and renaming the variables
of the relational formulas accordingly, one can consider that
\mathcal{Q}
simply
is
a unary formula.
20
20

20

Renaming can be minimized by assuming at the outset that the considered programs
c,c^{\prime}
are acting on separable parts of the store
[
BDR04
,
BDR11
]
, but some complications are inevitable for programs acting on the heap
[
Nau06
,
Ber11
]
.

In the present context, using shallow embedding of relations and assertions, let us postulate the following:
Any store relation
\mathcal{Q}
has an encoding
{{}^{\bullet}\!\mathcal{Q}}
as a predicate on variable stores
where the variables on which the considered program acts are “on the left”,
and an encoding
\mathcal{Q}^{\bullet}
where those variables are ‘‘on the right’’.
21
21

21

Such encodings are slightly tricky to formalize in our setting with stores as total maps on all variables.
We sketch the idea in terms of a simpler setting where stores are finite maps,
written like
\{x\mathord{:}1,y\mathord{:}7,z\mathord{:}12\}
. Suppose that for the relevant variables for the considered programs and specs are
x,y,\dots
. Assume some bijection to a disjoint set of variables
\hat{x},\hat{y},\dots
.
So a pair of stores, say
(\{x\mathord{:}1,y\mathord{:}2,z\mathord{:}3\},\{x\mathord{:}4,y\mathord{:}5,z\mathord{:}6\})

has encodings

{{}^{\bullet}\!(\{x\mathord{:}1,y\mathord{:}2,z\mathord{:}3\},\{x\mathord{:}4,y\mathord{:}5,z\mathord{:}6\})}\mathrel{\,\hat{=}\,}\{x\mathord{:}1,y\mathord{:}2,z\mathord{:}3,\hat{x}\mathord{:}4,\hat{y}\mathord{:}5,\hat{z}\mathord{:}6\}

and

(\{x\mathord{:}1,y\mathord{:}2,z\mathord{:}3\},\{x\mathord{:}4,y\mathord{:}5,z\mathord{:}6\})^{\bullet}\mathrel{\,\hat{=}\,}\{\hat{x}\mathord{:}1,\hat{y}\mathord{:}2,\hat{z}\mathord{:}3,x\mathord{:}4,y\mathord{:}5,z\mathord{:}6\}
.
Then
{{}^{\bullet}\!\mathcal{P}}\mathrel{\,\hat{=}\,}\{{{}^{\bullet}\!(s,t)}\mid(s,t)\in\mathcal{P}\}
.
To do something similar for total map stores,
one should restrict to finitely supported relations
\mathcal{P}
so
the definitions of
{{}^{\bullet}\!\mathcal{P}}
and
\mathcal{P}^{\bullet}
can exploit unused variables.

That is, the encodings satisfy the following, for all
c,\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q}
.

\begin{array}[t]{l}\models c\mathbin{\mid}\mathsf{skip}:\mathcal{P}\mathrel{\mbox{\footnotesize$\raisebox{-0.24113pt}{$\thickapprox$}\hskip-4.13332pt>$}}\mathcal{Q}\quad\mbox{iff}\quad\models c:{{}^{\bullet}\!\mathcal{P}}\leadsto{{}^{\bullet}\!\mathcal{Q}}\\
\models\mathsf{skip}\mathbin{\mid}c:\mathcal{P}\mathrel{\mbox{\footnotesize$\raisebox{-0.24113pt}{$\thickapprox$}\hskip-4.13332pt>$}}\mathcal{Q}\quad\mbox{iff}\quad\models c:\mathcal{P}^{\bullet}\leadsto\mathcal{Q}^{\bullet}\end{array}

(26)

To put these tedious details to work, we need the following which can be proved straightforwardly using semantic weakest preconditions or strongest postconditions. For later reference we introduce notation for weakest preconditions of program pairs:

\hbox{\pagecolor{light-gray}$\mbox{{wp}}(c\mathbin{\mid}c^{\prime})(\mathcal{R})$}\mathrel{\,\hat{=}\,}\{(s,s^{\prime})\mid\forall t,t^{\prime}.\>\llbracket\,c\,\rrbracket\,s\,t\land\llbracket\,c^{\prime}\,\rrbracket\,s^{\prime}\,t^{\prime}\Rightarrow(t,t^{\prime})\in\mathcal{R}\}
.
Note that

\models c\mathbin{\mid}c^{\prime}:\mathcal{P}\mathrel{\mbox{\footnotesize$\raisebox{-0.24113pt}{$\thickapprox$}\hskip-4.13332pt>$}}\mathcal{R}
iff

\mathcal{P}\Rightarrow\mbox{{wp}}(c\mathbin{\mid}c^{\prime})(\mathcal{R})
.

Lemma 27
.

For any
c,c^{\prime},\mathcal{P}
and
\mathcal{R}
, the following are equivalent:

(1)

\models c\mathbin{\mid}c^{\prime}:\mathcal{P}\mathrel{\mbox{\footnotesize$\raisebox{-0.24113pt}{$\thickapprox$}\hskip-4.13332pt>$}}\mathcal{R}

(2)

There is a
\mathcal{Q}
such that
\models c\mathbin{\mid}\mathsf{skip}:\mathcal{P}\mathrel{\mbox{\footnotesize$\raisebox{-0.24113pt}{$\thickapprox$}\hskip-4.13332pt>$}}\mathcal{Q}
and
\models\mathsf{skip}\mathbin{\mid}c^{\prime}:\mathcal{Q}\mathrel{\mbox{\footnotesize$\raisebox{-0.24113pt}{$\thickapprox$}\hskip-4.13332pt>$}}\mathcal{R}
.

(3)

There is a
\mathcal{Q}
such that
\models\mathsf{skip}\mathbin{\mid}c^{\prime}:\mathcal{P}\mathrel{\mbox{\footnotesize$\raisebox{-0.24113pt}{$\thickapprox$}\hskip-4.13332pt>$}}\mathcal{Q}
and
\models c\mathbin{\mid}\mathsf{skip}:\mathcal{Q}\mathrel{\mbox{\footnotesize$\raisebox{-0.24113pt}{$\thickapprox$}\hskip-4.13332pt>$}}\mathcal{R}
.

A direct consequence is the soundness of the following rules, which are analogous to

rLRseq
and
rRLseq
.

\frac{c:{{}^{\bullet}\!\mathcal{P}}\leadsto{{}^{\bullet}\!\mathcal{Q}}\quad c^{\prime}:\mathcal{Q}^{\bullet}\leadsto\mathcal{R}^{\bullet}}{c\mathbin{\mid}c^{\prime}:\mathcal{P}\mathrel{\mbox{\footnotesize$\raisebox{-0.24113pt}{$\thickapprox$}\hskip-4.13332pt>$}}\mathcal{R}}\quad\textsc{uLRseq}\par\frac{c^{\prime}:\mathcal{P}^{\bullet}\leadsto\mathcal{Q}^{\bullet}\quad c:{{}^{\bullet}\!\mathcal{Q}}\leadsto{{}^{\bullet}\!\mathcal{R}}}{c\mathbin{\mid}c^{\prime}:\mathcal{P}\mathrel{\mbox{\footnotesize$\raisebox{-0.24113pt}{$\thickapprox$}\hskip-4.13332pt>$}}\mathcal{R}}\quad\textsc{uRLseq}

Now we obtain a completeness result akin to those in the literature:
rule
uLRseq
, together with HL, comprises a Cook complete logic for relational judgments.
The proof is as follows.
Suppose

\models c\mathbin{\mid}c^{\prime}:\mathcal{P}\mathrel{\mbox{\footnotesize$\raisebox{-0.24113pt}{$\thickapprox$}\hskip-4.13332pt>$}}\mathcal{R}
is valid.
By Lemma
27
, there is a
\mathcal{Q}
such that

\models c\mathbin{\mid}\mathsf{skip}:\mathcal{P}\mathrel{\mbox{\footnotesize$\raisebox{-0.24113pt}{$\thickapprox$}\hskip-4.13332pt>$}}\mathcal{Q}
and

\models\mathsf{skip}\mathbin{\mid}c^{\prime}:\mathcal{Q}\mathrel{\mbox{\footnotesize$\raisebox{-0.24113pt}{$\thickapprox$}\hskip-4.13332pt>$}}\mathcal{R}
.
By (
26
) we have

\models c:{{}^{\bullet}\!\mathcal{P}}\leadsto{{}^{\bullet}\!\mathcal{Q}}
and

\models c^{\prime}:\mathcal{Q}^{\bullet}\leadsto\mathcal{R}^{\bullet}
.
By completeness of HL these judgments are provable.
Application of rule
rLRseq
proves

c\mathbin{\mid}c^{\prime}:\mathcal{P}\mathrel{\mbox{\footnotesize$\raisebox{-0.24113pt}{$\thickapprox$}\hskip-4.13332pt>$}}\mathcal{R}
.
A symmetric proof establishes Cook completeness of
uRLseq
plus HL.

In accord with the detour about embedding HL in RHL+, the preceding considerations lead to an alternate proof of
26
along the following lines.
First show that RHL+ is complete for one-sided judgments (since HL is).
Second, the rules
rSeq
and
rRewrite
suffice to derive

rRLseq
(in fact using only command equivalences of the form

c;\mathsf{skip}\mathrel{\simeq}c
and
\mathsf{skip};c\mathrel{\simeq}c
).
Finally, proceed by an argument similar to the preceding paragraph.

8.
The
\forall\exists
logic ERHL+

In this section, we consider a standalone deductive system for the

\mathrel{\mbox{\footnotesize$\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\exists}}{{\raisebox{-0.24113pt}{$\thickapprox$}\hskip-4.13332pt>}}$}}
judgment, called ERHL+, which involves only the
\mathrel{\mbox{\footnotesize$\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\exists}}{{\raisebox{-0.24113pt}{$\thickapprox$}\hskip-4.13332pt>}}$}}

judgment together with assertion validity and command equivalence
\mathrel{\simeq}

just like RHL+. Unary correctness is subsumed because

c:P\leadsto Q
is valid iff
c\mathbin{\mid}\mathsf{skip}:\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.0pt[$}P\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.1pt]$}\mathrel{\mbox{\footnotesize$\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\exists}}{{\raisebox{-0.24113pt}{$\thickapprox$}\hskip-4.13332pt>}}$}}\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.0pt[$}Q\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.1pt]$}
is
valid.
On the other side,

\mathsf{skip}\mathbin{\mid}c:\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$[\hskip-2.1pt\rangle$}P\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$]\hskip-2.0pt\rangle$}\mathrel{\mbox{\footnotesize$\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\exists}}{{\raisebox{-0.24113pt}{$\thickapprox$}\hskip-4.13332pt>}}$}}\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$[\hskip-2.1pt\rangle$}Q\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$]\hskip-2.0pt\rangle$}
is valid iff
the
forward underapproximation
judgment

c:P\mathrel{\mbox{\footnotesize$\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\exists}}{{\leadsto}}$}}Q
is valid.
This is defined by

\models c:P\mathrel{\mbox{\footnotesize$\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\exists}}{{\leadsto}}$}}Q
iff for any
s\in P
there exists
t\in Q
such that
\llbracket\,c\,\rrbracket\,s\,t
.

(27)

This has been called
possible correctness

[
Hoa78
]

and more recently the
existential
Hoare triple of
[
DYZD22
]
and the
sufficient incorrectness
triple of
[
ABGL24
]
.
22
22

22

O’Hearn’s incorrectness judgment
[
O’H20
]
—backwards underapproximation—
is similarly obtained by a backwards version of (
3
),
as noted in
[
AKL+23
]
.

\frac{}{\mathsf{skip}\mathbin{\mid}\mathsf{hav}\ {x}:(\dot{\exists}\mbox{\tiny$|$}x.\>\mathcal{P})\mathrel{\mbox{\footnotesize$\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\exists}}{{\raisebox{-0.24113pt}{$\thickapprox$}\hskip-4.13332pt>}}$}}\mathcal{P}}\hskip 8.50012pt\textsc{eSkipHav}\par\frac{}{\mathsf{hav}\ {x}\mathbin{\mid}\mathsf{skip}:(\dot{\forall}x\mbox{\tiny$|$}.\>\mathcal{P})\mathrel{\mbox{\footnotesize$\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\exists}}{{\raisebox{-0.24113pt}{$\thickapprox$}\hskip-4.13332pt>}}$}}\mathcal{P}}\hskip 8.50012pt\textsc{eHavSkip}\par\frac{{\begin{array}[]{l}c\mathbin{\mid}\mathsf{skip}:\mathcal{Q}\land\raisebox{0.8611pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.0pt[$}e\raisebox{0.8611pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.1pt]$}\land\mathcal{L}\mathrel{\mbox{\footnotesize$\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\exists}}{{\raisebox{-0.24113pt}{$\thickapprox$}\hskip-4.13332pt>}}$}}\mathcal{Q}\hskip V=v\mathrel{\mbox{\footnotesize$\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\exists}}{{\raisebox{-0.24113pt}{$\thickapprox$}\hskip-4.13332pt>}}$}}\mathcal{Q}\land V\prec v\hskip 8.50012pt\mbox{for all $e^{\prime}\mathrel{\shortrightarrow}c^{\prime}$ in $gcs^{\prime}$ and all $v\in D$}\hskip
