Appendix B
Axioms for normal form equivalence

For the sake of straightforward presentation, command equivalence has been
formulated in terms of a single fixed set of hypotheses that axiomatize simple
assignments and boolean expressions (Defs.
5.1
and
5.1
).
However, many useful equivalences require no hypotheses.
Only a few specific hypotheses are needed to prove the normal form theorem,
and in this section we spell those out.

Observe that if
c\mathbin{/}f\hookrightarrow gcs
then there is a finite set of instances of this relation that supports the fact, namely the normal forms of subprograms of
c
.
This enables us to define a set of axioms that are useful for reasoning about
a given program
c
and its normal form. The definition is parameterized on
c
and on the choice of a
pc
variable and final label.

{defi}

The
normal form axioms
,
\mbox{{nfax}}(pc,c,f)
,
comprises the following set of equations.

(diffTest):

\llparenthesis\,\mathord{?}i\,\rrparenthesis\mathbin{;}\llparenthesis\,\mathord{?}j\,\rrparenthesis=0
for

i
and
j
in
\mbox{{labs}}(c)\cup\{f\}
such that
i\neq j

(setTest):

\llparenthesis\,\mathord{!}i\,\rrparenthesis\mathbin{;}\llparenthesis\,\mathord{?}i\,\rrparenthesis=\llparenthesis\,\mathord{!}i\,\rrparenthesis
for
i
in
\mbox{{labs}}(c)\cup\{f\}

(totIf):

\mbox{$\lnot$}\llparenthesis\,\mbox{{enab}}(gcs)\,\rrparenthesis=0
for every
\mathsf{if}\ {gcs}\ \mathsf{fi}
that occurs in
c

(testCommuteAsgn):

\llparenthesis\,\mathord{?}i\,\rrparenthesis;\llparenthesis\,x:=e\,\rrparenthesis=\llparenthesis\,x:=e\,\rrparenthesis;\llparenthesis\,\mathord{?}i\,\rrparenthesis

for every
x:=e
in
c
such that
x\not\equiv pc
,
and every
i
in
\mbox{{labs}}(c)\cup\{f\}

(testCommuteHav):

\llparenthesis\,\mathord{?}i\,\rrparenthesis;\llparenthesis\,\mathsf{hav}\ {x}\,\rrparenthesis=\llparenthesis\,\mathsf{hav}\ {x}\,\rrparenthesis;\llparenthesis\,\mathord{?}i\,\rrparenthesis

for every
\mathsf{hav}\ {x}
in
c
such that
x\not\equiv pc
,
and every
i
in
\mbox{{labs}}(c)\cup\{f\}

Lemma 37
.

For any
pc,c,f
, the equations
\mbox{{nfax}}(pc,c,f)

follow by KAT reasoning from the equations of
Hyp
.

Proof B.1
.

Observe that, in light of Lemma
13
,
the set
Hyp
can characterized in terms of
\mathfrak{L}
as follows:

•

\llparenthesis\,e\,\rrparenthesis=0
for every boolean expression
e

such that
\llparenthesis\,e\,\rrparenthesis^{\mathfrak{L}}=\emptyset

•

\llparenthesis\,e_{0}\,\rrparenthesis;\llparenthesis\,x:=e\,\rrparenthesis;\mbox{$\lnot$}\llparenthesis\,e_{1}\,\rrparenthesis=0

for every
x,e,e_{0},e_{1}
such that

\llparenthesis\,e_{0}\,\rrparenthesis^{\mathfrak{L}};\llparenthesis\,x:=e\,\rrparenthesis^{\mathfrak{L}};\mbox{$\lnot$}\llparenthesis\,e_{1}\,\rrparenthesis^{\mathfrak{L}}=\emptyset
.

•

\llparenthesis\,e_{0}\,\rrparenthesis;\llparenthesis\,\mathsf{hav}\ {x}\,\rrparenthesis;\mbox{$\lnot$}\llparenthesis\,e_{1}\,\rrparenthesis=0

for every
x,e,e_{0},e_{1}
such that

\llparenthesis\,e_{0}\,\rrparenthesis^{\mathfrak{L}};\llparenthesis\,\mathsf{hav}\ {x}\,\rrparenthesis^{\mathfrak{L}};\mbox{$\lnot$}\llparenthesis\,e_{1}\,\rrparenthesis^{\mathfrak{L}}=\emptyset
.

Now we proceed.

(diffTest)
If
i\neq j
then
\llparenthesis\,\mathord{?}i\land\mathord{?}j\,\rrparenthesis^{\mathfrak{L}}=\emptyset
,
so
Hyp
contains
\llparenthesis\,\mathord{?}i\land\mathord{?}j\,\rrparenthesis=0
.
So we have
\llparenthesis\,\mathord{?}i\,\rrparenthesis\mathbin{;}\llparenthesis\,\mathord{?}j\,\rrparenthesis=\llparenthesis\,\mathord{?}i\land\mathord{?}j\,\rrparenthesis=0

using the definition of
\llparenthesis\,-\,\rrparenthesis
.

(setTest)

Hyp
contains
\llparenthesis\,\mathsf{skip}\,\rrparenthesis;\llparenthesis\,\mathord{!}i\,\rrparenthesis\mathbin{;}\mbox{$\lnot$}\llparenthesis\,\mathord{?}i\,\rrparenthesis=0
,
and
\llparenthesis\,\mathsf{skip}\,\rrparenthesis
is
1
.
Now observe that

\llparenthesis\,\mathord{!}i\,\rrparenthesis=\llparenthesis\,\mathord{!}i\,\rrparenthesis\mathbin{;}(\llparenthesis\,\mathord{?}i\,\rrparenthesis+\mbox{$\lnot$}\llparenthesis\,\mathord{?}i\,\rrparenthesis)=\llparenthesis\,\mathord{!}i\,\rrparenthesis\mathbin{;}\llparenthesis\,\mathord{?}i\,\rrparenthesis+1;\llparenthesis\,\mathord{!}i\,\rrparenthesis\mathbin{;}\mbox{$\lnot$}\llparenthesis\,\mathord{?}i\,\rrparenthesis=\llparenthesis\,\mathord{!}i\,\rrparenthesis\mathbin{;}\llparenthesis\,\mathord{?}i\,\rrparenthesis

using KAT laws and
1;\llparenthesis\,\mathord{!}i\,\rrparenthesis\mathbin{;}\mbox{$\lnot$}\llparenthesis\,\mathord{?}i\,\rrparenthesis=0
.

(totIf)
If
\mathsf{if}\ {gcs}\ \mathsf{fi}
that occurs in
c
then
we have
(\mbox{$\lnot$}\llparenthesis\,\mbox{{enab}}(gcs)\,\rrparenthesis)^{\mathfrak{L}}=\emptyset

as a consequence of the
total
-
if
condition (Def.
3
).
So the equation
\mbox{$\lnot$}\llparenthesis\,\mbox{{enab}}(gcs)\,\rrparenthesis=0
is in
Hyp
.

(testCommuteAsgn)
The equation

\llparenthesis\,\mathord{?}i\,\rrparenthesis;\llparenthesis\,x:=e\,\rrparenthesis=\llparenthesis\,x:=e\,\rrparenthesis;\llparenthesis\,\mathord{?}i\,\rrparenthesis
,
is equivalent to the conjunction of

\llparenthesis\,\mathord{?}i\,\rrparenthesis;\llparenthesis\,x:=e\,\rrparenthesis;\mbox{$\lnot$}\llparenthesis\,\mathord{?}i\,\rrparenthesis=0

and
\mbox{$\lnot$}\llparenthesis\,\mathord{?}i\,\rrparenthesis;\llparenthesis\,x:=e\,\rrparenthesis;\llparenthesis\,\mathord{?}i\,\rrparenthesis=0

using KAT laws.
By definition of
\llparenthesis\,-\,\rrparenthesis
and boolean algebra,

\mbox{$\lnot$}\llparenthesis\,\mathord{?}i\,\rrparenthesis;\llparenthesis\,x:=e\,\rrparenthesis;\llparenthesis\,\mathord{?}i\,\rrparenthesis=0

is equivalent to

\llparenthesis\,\mbox{$\lnot$}\mathord{?}i\,\rrparenthesis;\llparenthesis\,x:=e\,\rrparenthesis;\mbox{$\lnot$}\llparenthesis\,\mbox{$\lnot$}\mathord{?}i\,\rrparenthesis=0
.
Both
\mathord{?}i\Rightarrow{(\mathord{?}i)}^{x}_{e}
and

\mbox{$\lnot$}\mathord{?}i\Rightarrow{(\mbox{$\lnot$}\mathord{?}i)}^{x}_{e}

are valid because
pc\not\equiv x
,
so
Hyp
contains both equations

\llparenthesis\,\mathord{?}i\,\rrparenthesis;\llparenthesis\,x:=e\,\rrparenthesis;\mbox{$\lnot$}\llparenthesis\,\mathord{?}i\,\rrparenthesis=0

and

\llparenthesis\,\mbox{$\lnot$}\mathord{?}i\,\rrparenthesis;\llparenthesis\,x:=e\,\rrparenthesis;\mbox{$\lnot$}\llparenthesis\,\mbox{$\lnot$}\mathord{?}i\,\rrparenthesis=0
.

(testCommuteHav)
The equation

\llparenthesis\,\mathord{?}i\,\rrparenthesis;\llparenthesis\,\mathsf{hav}\ {x}\,\rrparenthesis=\llparenthesis\,\mathsf{hav}\ {x}\,\rrparenthesis;\llparenthesis\,\mathord{?}i\,\rrparenthesis
,
is equivalent to the conjunction of

\llparenthesis\,\mathord{?}i\,\rrparenthesis;\llparenthesis\,\mathsf{hav}\ {x}\,\rrparenthesis;\mbox{$\lnot$}\llparenthesis\,\mathord{?}i\,\rrparenthesis=0

and
\mbox{$\lnot$}\llparenthesis\,\mathord{?}i\,\rrparenthesis;\llparenthesis\,\mathsf{hav}\ {x}\,\rrparenthesis;\llparenthesis\,\mathord{?}i\,\rrparenthesis=0

using KAT laws.
By definition of
\llparenthesis\,-\,\rrparenthesis
and boolean algebra,

\mbox{$\lnot$}\llparenthesis\,\mathord{?}i\,\rrparenthesis;\llparenthesis\,\mathsf{hav}\ {x}\,\rrparenthesis;\llparenthesis\,\mathord{?}i\,\rrparenthesis=0

is equivalent to

\llparenthesis\,\mbox{$\lnot$}\mathord{?}i\,\rrparenthesis;\llparenthesis\,\mathsf{hav}\ {x}\,\rrparenthesis;\mbox{$\lnot$}\llparenthesis\,\mbox{$\lnot$}\mathord{?}i\,\rrparenthesis=0
.
Both
\llparenthesis\,\mathord{?}i\,\rrparenthesis;\llparenthesis\,\mathsf{hav}\ {x}\,\rrparenthesis;\mbox{$\lnot$}\llparenthesis\,\mathord{?}i\,\rrparenthesis=0
and

\llparenthesis\,\mbox{$\lnot$}\mathord{?}i\,\rrparenthesis;\llparenthesis\,\mathsf{hav}\ {x}\,\rrparenthesis;\mbox{$\lnot$}\llparenthesis\,\mbox{$\lnot$}\mathord{?}i\,\rrparenthesis=0

are instances of the form
\llparenthesis\,e\,\rrparenthesis;\llparenthesis\,\mathsf{hav}\ {x}\,\rrparenthesis;\mbox{$\lnot$}\llparenthesis\,e\,\rrparenthesis=0
,
and we are assuming
x\not\equiv pc
so they are both in
Hyp
and we are done.

Remark 38
.

\mbox{{nfax}}(pc,c,f)
is finite, and every equation is equivalent to one of the form
\mathit{KE}=0
.
So entailments of the form
\mbox{{nfax}}(pc,c,f)\vdash\mathit{KE}_{0}=\mathit{KE}_{1}

are decidable in PSPACE
[
CKS96
]
.
∎

Lemma 39
.

All equations in
\mbox{{nfax}}(pc,c,f)
are true in
\mathfrak{L}
, for any
pc,c,f
.

This holds because the equations follow from
Hyp
(Lemma
37
)
and the equations in
Hyp
are true in
\mathfrak{L}
(Lemma
15
).
Note that
c
need not be
ok
for this result.
However, truth of (totIf) does depend on the
total
-
if
condition (Def.
3
).

Lemma 40
.

The following hold for any
c,pc,f
such that
pc
does not occur in
c
.

(diffTestNeg)

\mbox{{nfax}}(pc,c,f)\vdash\llparenthesis\,\mathord{?}i\,\rrparenthesis=\llparenthesis\,\mathord{?}i\,\rrparenthesis;\mbox{$\lnot$}\llparenthesis\,\mathord{?}j\,\rrparenthesis

and
\llparenthesis\,\mathord{?}i\,\rrparenthesis\leq\mbox{$\lnot$}\llparenthesis\,\mathord{?}j\,\rrparenthesis

for
i\neq j
with
i,j
in
\mbox{{labs}}(c)\cup\{f\}
.

(nf-enab-labs)

\mbox{{nfax}}(pc,c,f)\vdash\llparenthesis\,\mbox{{enab}}(gcs)\,\rrparenthesis=\llparenthesis\,(\lor\,i\>:\>i\in\mbox{{labs}}(c)\>:\>\mathord{?}i)\,\rrparenthesis

if
\mbox{{okf}}(c,f)
and
c\mathbin{/}f\hookrightarrow gcs
.

(nf-lab-enab)

\mbox{{nfax}}(pc,c,f)\vdash\llparenthesis\,\mathord{?}\mbox{{lab}}(c)\,\rrparenthesis\leq\llparenthesis\,\mbox{{enab}}(gcs)\,\rrparenthesis

if
\mbox{{okf}}(c,f)
and
c\mathbin{/}f\hookrightarrow gcs
.

(nf-enab-disj)

\mbox{{nfax}}(pc,c,f)\vdash\llparenthesis\,\mbox{{enab}}(gcs_{0})\,\rrparenthesis;\llparenthesis\,\mbox{{enab}}(gcs_{1})\,\rrparenthesis=0

if
\mbox{{okf}}(c,f)
and
gcs_{0}
and
gcs_{1}
are the normal form bodies of disjoint subprograms of
c
.

(nf-enab-corr)

\mbox{{nfax}}(pc,c,f)\vdash\llparenthesis\,\mbox{{enab}}(gcs)\,\rrparenthesis;\llparenthesis\,gcs\,\rrparenthesis=\llparenthesis\,\mbox{{enab}}(gcs)\,\rrparenthesis;\llparenthesis\,gcs\,\rrparenthesis;(\llparenthesis\,\mbox{{enab}}(gcs)\,\rrparenthesis+\llparenthesis\,\mathord{?}f\,\rrparenthesis)

if
\mbox{{okf}}(c,f)
and
c\mathbin{/}f\hookrightarrow gcs
and
pc\notin vars(c)
.

(nf-enab-corr) can be abbreviated

\mbox{{nfax}}(pc,c,f)\vdash\llparenthesis\,gcs\,\rrparenthesis:\llparenthesis\,\mbox{{enab}}(gcs)\,\rrparenthesis\leadsto\llparenthesis\,\mbox{{enab}}(gcs)\,\rrparenthesis+\llparenthesis\,\mathord{?}f\,\rrparenthesis
.

Proof B.2
.

(diffTestNeg) follows from (diffTest) using boolean algebra.

(nf-enab-labs)
By rule induction on the normal form judgment.

For
\mathsf{do}^{n}\ {gcs_{0}}\ \mathsf{od}
, the normal form body is explicitly defined to include,
not only a command with guard
\mathord{?}n\land e
for each
e\mathrel{\shortrightarrow}d
in
gcs_{0}

but also a command with guard
\mathord{?}n\land\mbox{$\lnot$}\mbox{{enab}}(\ldots)
for their complement.
The disjunction of their enabling conditions simplifies to
\mathord{?}n
.
By the normal form rule and induction hypothesis, the enabling condition for the collected normal form bodies is the disjunction over their label tests, hence the result.

For
\mathsf{if}^{n}\ {gcs_{0}}\ \mathsf{fi}
, the normal form body includes disjuncts

\mathord{?}n\land e
for each of the guard expressions
e
in
gcs_{0}
.
Consider the case of two branches, i.e.,
gcs_{0}
is
e_{0}\mathrel{\shortrightarrow}d_{0}\talloblong e_{1}\mathrel{\shortrightarrow}d_{1}
,
the disjunction is
(\mathord{?}n\land e_{0})\lor(\mathord{?}n\land e_{1})
.
Observe

\begin{array}[]{lll}&\llparenthesis\,(\mathord{?}n\land e_{0})\lor(\mathord{?}n\land e_{1})\,\rrparenthesis\\
=&\llparenthesis\,\mathord{?}n\,\rrparenthesis;\llparenthesis\,e_{0}\,\rrparenthesis+\llparenthesis\,\mathord{?}n\,\rrparenthesis;\llparenthesis\,e_{1}\,\rrparenthesis&\mbox{def $\llparenthesis\,-\,\rrparenthesis$}\\
=&\llparenthesis\,\mathord{?}n\,\rrparenthesis;(\llparenthesis\,e_{0}\,\rrparenthesis+\llparenthesis\,e_{1}\,\rrparenthesis)&\mbox{KAT law}\\
=&\llparenthesis\,\mathord{?}n\,\rrparenthesis;\llparenthesis\,e_{0}\lor e_{1}\,\rrparenthesis&\mbox{def $\llparenthesis\,-\,\rrparenthesis$}\\
=&\llparenthesis\,\mathord{?}n\,\rrparenthesis&\mbox{axiom (totIf), unit law}\end{array}

The rest of the argument is like for
\mathsf{do}
.
The general case (multiple branches) is similar.

(nf-lab-enab) follows from (nf-enab-labs), using boolean algebra and
definition of
\llparenthesis\,-\,\rrparenthesis
(distributing over
\lor
).

(nf-enab-disj) is proved by induction on normal form,
using that by
ok
subprograms have distinct labels,
and (nf-enab-labs) and (diffTest).

(nf-enab-corr)
The proof is by induction on the normal form relation and by calculation
using KAT laws and the axioms.
It uses (testCommuteAsgn) so
pc
must not occur in
c
.

See
22

Proof B.3
.

Follows by definitions from provability lemma (nf-enab-labs) of Lemma
40
,
using Lemmas
39
and
13
.

Appendix C
Normal form bodies

\par\frac{c\mathbin{/}\mbox{{lab}}(d)\hookrightarrow gcs_{0}\quad d\mathbin{/}f\hookrightarrow gcs_{1}}{c;d\mathbin{/}f\hookrightarrow gcs_{0}\talloblong gcs_{1}}\par\par\frac{\mbox{{bodies}}(gcs)\mathbin{/}f\hookrightarrow\mathit{nfs}}{\mathsf{if}^{n}\ {gcs}\ \mathsf{fi}\mathbin{/}f\hookrightarrow map((\lambda(e\mathrel{\shortrightarrow}c)\,.\,\mathord{?}n\land e\mathrel{\shortrightarrow}\mathord{!}\mbox{{lab}}(c)),\,gcs)\talloblong\mbox{{concat}}(\mathit{nfs})}\par\par\frac{\mbox{{bodies}}(gcs)\mathbin{/}f\hookrightarrow\mathit{nfs}}{\mathsf{do}^{n}\ {gcs}\ \mathsf{od}\mathbin{/}f\hookrightarrow map((\lambda(e\mathrel{\shortrightarrow}c)\,.\,\mathord{?}n\land e\mathrel{\shortrightarrow}\mathord{!}\mbox{{lab}}(c)),\,gcs)\talloblong\mathord{?}n\land\mbox{$\lnot$}\mbox{{enab}}(gcs)\mathrel{\shortrightarrow}\mathord{!}f\talloblong\mbox{{concat}}(\mathit{nfs})}\par\frac{c\mathbin{/}f\hookrightarrow gcs}{[c]\mathbin{/}f\hookrightarrow[gcs]}\par\frac{c\mathbin{/}f\hookrightarrow gcs\quad cs\mathbin{/}f\hookrightarrow\mathit{nfs}}{c::cs\mathbin{/}f\hookrightarrow gcs::\mathit{nfs}}\par

Figure 24.
Normal form bodies for if and do.

Figure 24
gives the general cases of normal forms for if- and do-commands, which subsume the special cases given in
Figure 13
.
The definition of normal form bodies for commands is mutually inductive
with the definition of normal form body list for nonempty command lists,
which is given by the last two rules in
Figure 24
.
The rules use square brackets for singleton list and
::
for list cons.
They use
\mbox{{bodies}}(gcs)
and
\mbox{{concat}}(nfs)
defined by

\begin{array}[]{lll}\mbox{{bodies}}(e\mathrel{\shortrightarrow}c)&=&[c]\\
\mbox{{bodies}}(e\mathrel{\shortrightarrow}c\talloblong gcs)&=&c::\mbox{{bodies}}(gcs)\\
\mbox{{concat}}([gcs])&=&gcs\\
\mbox{{concat}}(gcs:nfs)&=&gcs\talloblong\mbox{{concat}}(nfs)\end{array}

Appendix D
Additional proofs

See
31

Proof D.1
.

We prove the main statement by mutual implication, and afterwards consider the issue of finite support.

(right implies left)
If
an
is a valid annotation of
\prod(A,A^{\prime},L,R,J,K)
for
\mathcal{P}\mathrel{\mbox{\footnotesize$\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\exists}}{{\raisebox{-0.24113pt}{$\thickapprox$}\hskip-4.13332pt>}}$}}\mathcal{Q}

and
K
is an adequate filtering for
an
,
then by Lemma
30
the product is
\mathcal{P}
-adequate.
Since
an
is valid for
\mathcal{P}\leadsto\mathcal{Q}
we have

\prod(A,A^{\prime},L,R,J,K)\models\mathcal{P}\leadsto\mathcal{Q}
by Lemma
3
.
So by Lemma
29
we have
A,A^{\prime}\models\mathcal{P}\mathrel{\mbox{\footnotesize$\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\exists}}{{\raisebox{-0.24113pt}{$\thickapprox$}\hskip-4.13332pt>}}$}}\mathcal{Q}
.

(left implies right)
Suppose
A,A^{\prime}\models\mathcal{P}\mathrel{\mbox{\footnotesize$\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\exists}}{{\raisebox{-0.24113pt}{$\thickapprox$}\hskip-4.13332pt>}}$}}\mathcal{Q}
.
The idea is to use a left-first sequential alignment automaton such that,
once the left-only execution has finished, with final store
t
,
the filter condition only keeps states that are in some terminating run of

A^{\prime}
that ends in a store
t^{\prime}
such that
(t,t^{\prime})\in\mathcal{Q}
.

To make this precise we need a few definitions.
Let
\mbox{{Traces}}(A)
be the set of all nonempty and finite sequences of states of
automata
A
that are consecutive under
A
’s transition relation. In what
follows, we use
\alpha,\beta
to range over traces, and write

\mathit{start}(\alpha)
and
\mathit{end}(\alpha)
for the first and last
states of
\alpha
.
For
s\in Sto
,
n^{\prime}\in Ctrl^{\prime}
, and
s^{\prime}\in Sto^{\prime}
,
define
\mathit{termQtr}(s,n^{\prime},s^{\prime})
to be the set of terminated traces of
A^{\prime}

that begin at
(n^{\prime},s^{\prime})
and end in a state
t^{\prime}
for which
(s,t^{\prime})\in\mathcal{Q}
.
That is,

\mathit{termQtr}(s,n^{\prime},s^{\prime})\mathrel{\,\hat{=}\,}\{\alpha\in\mbox{{Traces}}(A^{\prime})\mid\mathit{start}(\alpha)=(n^{\prime},s^{\prime})\land\exists t^{\prime}.\>\mathit{end}(\alpha)=(\mathit{fin}^{\prime},t^{\prime})\land(s,t^{\prime})\in\mathcal{Q}\}.

Define the restriction to traces of minimum length, as follows.
29
29

29

One
may wonder how
\min
is defined, in case
\mathit{termQtr}(s,n^{\prime},s^{\prime})
is empty
(and hence so is
\mbox{{map}}(\mbox{{len}},\mathit{termQtr}(s,n^{\prime},s^{\prime}))
),
but that does not matter because in this case
\mathit{shortQtr}(s,n^{\prime},s^{\prime})
is empty.

\mathit{shortQtr}(s,n^{\prime},s^{\prime})\mathrel{\,\hat{=}\,}\{\alpha\in\mathit{termQtr}(s,n^{\prime},s^{\prime})\mid\mbox{{len}}(\alpha)=\min(\mbox{{map}}(\mbox{{len}},\mathit{termQtr}(s,n^{\prime},s^{\prime})))\}

We need the following facts:

(i)

Suppose
\alpha
is in
\mathit{termQtr}(t,m^{\prime},s^{\prime})

and
(n^{\prime},t^{\prime})
is in
\alpha
.
Then
\mathit{termQtr}(t,n^{\prime},t^{\prime})
is nonempty.

(ii)

\mathit{termQtr}(t,n^{\prime},s^{\prime})
is nonempty
iff
\mathit{shortQtr}(t,n^{\prime},s^{\prime})
is nonempty.

(iii)

If
(s,s^{\prime})
is in
\mathcal{P}
and there is an
A
-trace from
(\mathit{init},s)
to
(\mathit{fin},t)
,
then
\mathit{termQtr}(t,init^{\prime},s^{\prime})\neq\emptyset
.

Both (i) and (ii) follow directly by definitions.
Fact (iii) is a consequence of the theorem’s assumption
that
A,A^{\prime}\models\mathcal{P}\mathrel{\mbox{\footnotesize$\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\exists}}{{\raisebox{-0.24113pt}{$\thickapprox$}\hskip-4.13332pt>}}$}}\mathcal{Q}
.
If
(s,s^{\prime})\in\mathcal{P}
and there is an
A
-trace from
(\mathit{init},s)
to
(\mathit{fin},t)

then by
A,A^{\prime}\models\mathcal{P}\mathrel{\mbox{\footnotesize$\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\exists}}{{\raisebox{-0.24113pt}{$\thickapprox$}\hskip-4.13332pt>}}$}}\mathcal{Q}
there is some

t^{\prime}
and
A^{\prime}
-trace
\alpha
from
(\mathit{init}^{\prime},s^{\prime})
to
(\mathit{fin}^{\prime},t^{\prime})

with
(t,t^{\prime})\in\mathcal{Q}
.
By definition,
\alpha
is in
\mathit{termQtr}(t,\mathit{init}^{\prime},s^{\prime})
.

Next we define
L,R,J
as needed for left-first sequential alignment product.
So we let
J\mathrel{\,\hat{=}\,}\emptyset
.
In case the transition relations
\mapsto,\mapsto^{\prime}
of
A,A^{\prime}
are total (as in the case of program automata) it suffices to define

L\mathrel{\,\hat{=}\,}[*|\mathit{init}^{\prime}]\land\mbox{$\lnot$}[\mathit{fin}|*]
and

R\mathrel{\,\hat{=}\,}[\mathit{fin}|*]\land\mbox{$\lnot$}[\mathit{fin}|\mathit{fin}^{\prime}]
.
In general, to ensure liveness in the sense required by Def.
4.1
,
we need

\begin{array}[]{l}L\mathrel{\,\hat{=}\,}[*|\mathit{init}^{\prime}]\land\mbox{$\lnot$}[\mathit{fin}|*]\land(\mbox{{dom}}(\mapsto)\times states^{\prime})\\
R\mathrel{\,\hat{=}\,}[\mathit{fin}|*]\land\mbox{$\lnot$}[\mathit{fin}|\mathit{fin}^{\prime}]\land(states\times\mbox{{dom}}(\mapsto^{\prime}))\end{array}

Define
K
by
30
30

30

It also works to define
K
using shortest traces only.

((n,n^{\prime}),(s,s^{\prime}))\in K\quad\mathrel{\,\hat{=}\,}\quad n^{\prime}=\mathit{init}^{\prime}\lor(n=\mathit{fin}\land\mathit{termQtr}(s,n^{\prime},s^{\prime})\neq\emptyset)

Let
an
be the canonical semantic annotation for
\mathcal{P}
,
i.e.,
an(n,n^{\prime})
is the strongest invariant of

\prod(A,A^{\prime},L,R,J,K)
with respect to
\mathcal{P}
.
To be precise, for any
n,n^{\prime}
:

an(n,n^{\prime})\mathrel{\,\hat{=}\,}\{(t,t^{\prime})\mid\exists s,s^{\prime}.\>(s,s^{\prime})\in\mathcal{P}\land((\mathit{init},\mathit{init}^{\prime}),(s,s^{\prime}))\Mapsto_{K}^{*}((n,n^{\prime}),(t,t^{\prime}))\}

By construction, this annotation satisfies
\mathcal{P}\Rightarrow an(\mathit{init},\mathit{init}^{\prime})

and moreover it satisfies the verification conditions, i.e., it is valid.
It remains to show that
an(\mathit{fin},\mathit{fin}^{\prime})\Rightarrow\mathcal{Q}
(i.e., it is an annotation for
\mathcal{P}\leadsto\mathcal{Q}
).
By definition of
K
, any stores
t,t^{\prime}
for which
((\mathit{fin},\mathit{fin}^{\prime}),(t,t^{\prime}))

is reachable via
\Mapsto_{K}
satisfy
\mathcal{Q}
.
Because
an(\mathit{fin},\mathit{fin}^{\prime})
is exactly these stores,
we conclude that
an(\mathit{fin},\mathit{fin}^{\prime})\Rightarrow\mathcal{Q}
.

It remains to show
K
is an adequate filtering for
an
.

For the enabled condition in the Def.
9.1
, we need
for any
n,n^{\prime}
,
\breve{an}(n,n^{\prime})\Rightarrow L\lor R\lor J\lor[\mathit{fin}|\mathit{fin}^{\prime}]
.
By construction, an invariant of the product is

[*|\mathit{init}^{\prime}]\lor[\mathit{fin}|*]
.
So by definition of
an
we get

\breve{an}(n,n^{\prime})\Rightarrow L\lor R\lor J\lor[\mathit{fin}|\mathit{fin}^{\prime}]
for any
n,n^{\prime}
.

The left-permissiveness condition in Def.
9.1

holds because
K
allows all left steps.
Joint-productivity holds because
J
is empty.
To show right-productivity we need a measure
V
.

To define
V
, first observe that all elements of
\mathit{shortQtr}(t,n^{\prime},s^{\prime})
have the same length,
if there are any elements.
So we can write
\mbox{{len}}(\mathit{shortQtr}(t,n^{\prime},s^{\prime}))
for that number, provided the set is not empty.
Now define
V
, a function from product states to naturals, by

\begin{array}[]{lcll}V((n,n^{\prime}),(s,s^{\prime}))&=&0&\mbox{if $n\neq\mathit{fin}$}\\
V((\mathit{fin},n^{\prime}),(t,s^{\prime}))&=&\mbox{{len}}(\mathit{shortQtr}(t,n^{\prime},s^{\prime}))&\mbox{if $\mathit{shortQtr}(t,n^{\prime},s^{\prime})\neq\emptyset$}\\
V((\mathit{fin},n^{\prime}),(t,s^{\prime}))&=&0&\mbox{otherwise}\end{array}

For right productivity, consider any product state
((n,n^{\prime}),(t,t^{\prime}))

and assume
(t,t^{\prime})\in an(n,n^{\prime})
and

((n,n^{\prime}),(t,t^{\prime}))\in R
.
We must show there is some
m^{\prime}
and
u^{\prime}
, and a transition
from
(n^{\prime},t^{\prime})\mapsto^{\prime}(m^{\prime},u^{\prime})

such that

•

((n,m^{\prime}),(t,u^{\prime}))\in K(n,m^{\prime})
, and

•

V((n,m^{\prime}),(t,u^{\prime}))<V((n,n^{\prime}),(t,t^{\prime}))

Observe that in general, from
(t,t^{\prime})\in an(n,n^{\prime})
we have that
((n,n^{\prime}),(t,t^{\prime}))
is reachable from some
((\mathit{init},\mathit{init}^{\prime}),(s,s^{\prime}))
with
(s,s^{\prime})\in\mathcal{P}
.

From
((n,n^{\prime}),(t,t^{\prime}))\in R
we have that
n=\mathit{fin}
and
n^{\prime}\neq\mathit{fin}^{\prime}
.
Now, in general
K\lor[\mathit{init}|\mathit{init}^{\prime}]
is an invariant of an LRJK-automaton,
so because
\mathit{fin}\neq\mathit{init}
we have that
\breve{an}(\mathit{fin},n^{\prime})
implies
K(\mathit{fin},n^{\prime})
.
In particular we have
((\mathit{fin},n^{\prime}),(t,t^{\prime}))
is in
K(\mathit{fin},n^{\prime})
.
Thus by definition of
K
we have that
\mathit{termQtr}(t,n^{\prime},t^{\prime})\neq\emptyset
,
unless
n^{\prime}=\mathit{init}^{\prime}
.
In case
n^{\prime}=\mathit{init}^{\prime}
we get
\mathit{termQtr}(t,\mathit{init}^{\prime},t^{\prime})\neq\emptyset
as follows.
By the general observation above,
from
(t,t^{\prime})\in an(\mathit{fin},\mathit{init}^{\prime})
we have that
((\mathit{fin},\mathit{init}^{\prime}),(t,t^{\prime}))
is reachable from some
((\mathit{init},\mathit{init}^{\prime}),(s,s^{\prime}))
with
(s,s^{\prime})\in\mathcal{P}
.
This gives a terminated
A
-trace. Now we can appeal to
fact (iii) above to get
\mathit{termQtr}(t,\mathit{init}^{\prime},t^{\prime})\neq\emptyset
.

Now by fact (ii) we have
\mathit{shortQtr}(t,n^{\prime},t^{\prime})
is nonempty.
Choose any
\alpha
in
\mathit{shortQtr}(t,n^{\prime},t^{\prime})
.
The first state of
\alpha
is
(n^{\prime},t^{\prime})
, by definitions,
and
\mbox{{len}}(\alpha)>1
because
n^{\prime}\neq\mathit{fin}^{\prime}
.
Let
(m^{\prime},u^{\prime})
be the second state of
\alpha
,
so we have
(n^{\prime},t^{\prime})\mapsto^{\prime}(m^{\prime},u^{\prime})
.
Using facts (i) and (ii), the set
\mathit{termQtr}(t,m^{\prime},u^{\prime})
is nonempty,
so by definition of
K
we have
((\mathit{fin},m^{\prime}),(t,u^{\prime}))\in K(\mathit{fin},m^{\prime})
, proving the first bullet above.

Since
\mathit{shortQtr}(t,n^{\prime},t^{\prime})
is nonempty (and traces have nonzero length)
we have
V((\mathit{fin},n^{\prime}),(t,t^{\prime}))\neq 0
.
Since
(m^{\prime},u^{\prime})
is the second state in
\alpha
which is in
\mathit{shortQtr}(t,n^{\prime},t^{\prime})
,
we have
V((\mathit{fin},m^{\prime}),(t,u^{\prime}))=V((\mathit{fin},n^{\prime}),(t,t^{\prime}))-1

whence

V((\mathit{fin},m^{\prime}),(t,u^{\prime}))<V((\mathit{fin},n^{\prime}),(t,t^{\prime}))
, proving the second bullet above and completing the proof of the main statement of the theorem.

Concerning finite support, suppose the stores of
A
and
A^{\prime}
are
variable stores, and suppose
A,A^{\prime},\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q}
are finitely supported.
We must show that
L,R,J,K,an,V
are too.
Let
X
be all the variables on which
\mathcal{P}
or
\mathcal{Q}
depend (on the left or right), together with all the variables in the support of the transition relation of
A
or
A^{\prime}
.
Note that in any trace
\alpha
of either automaton, any variable
y\notin X
is unchanged through the trace. Moreover if
\beta
is obtained from
\alpha
by setting
y
to another fixed value, then
\beta
is a trace of the automaton. Because
X
supports
\mathcal{P}
, we have that strongest invariants,
are supported by
X
; in brief,
an
is supported by
X
.
Because
X
supports
\mathcal{Q}
, each set
\mathit{termQtr}(s,n^{\prime},s^{\prime})
is determined by the projections of
s
and
s^{\prime}
on
X
, and the same for
\mathit{shortQtr}(s,n^{\prime},s^{\prime})
.
Thus the condition

\mathit{termQtr}(s,n^{\prime},s^{\prime})\neq\emptyset
in the definition of
K
is supported by
X
,
so
K
is as well.
We have
L,R,J
supported by
X
because
\mbox{{dom}}(\mapsto)
and
\mbox{{dom}}(\mapsto^{\prime})
are by assumption.
Finally,
V
is supported by
X
because the condition
\mathit{shortQtr}(s,n^{\prime},s^{\prime})\neq\emptyset
in its definition is.

Remark 41
.

A natural question is whether the
L,R,J,K
conditions, annotation
an
,
and measure
V
used in the proof of
31
are expressible in a first order assertion language.
Prior works on Hoare logic showed expressivity of weakest conditions in Peano arithmetic, based on Gödel encodings of stores, execution traces, etc.
[
AdBO09
,
dBdBZ80
]
as needed for
an,K,V
.
Thus Peano arithmetic suffices for our result.
∎

See
34

Proof D.2
.

The proof of this theorem proceeds in the same way as the proof of

25
. As in
25
, the proof uses
only relational assertions derived from
an(i,j)
,
L
,
R
, and
K
.

We start by choosing a variable
pc
that is
fresh with respect to
\mathcal{S}
,
\mathcal{T}
,
c
,
c^{\prime}
,
an
,
L
,
R
,
J
,
K
, and the
V
that
witnesses adequacy assumption (c).
Existence of such a variable is ensured by the assumption (d) of finite support.
By Lemma
20
there are
gcs
and
gcs^{\prime}
such that

c\mathbin{/}f\hookrightarrow gcs
and
c^{\prime}\mathbin{/}f^{\prime}\hookrightarrow gcs^{\prime}
. Let
n=\mbox{{lab}}(c)

and
n^{\prime}=\mbox{{lab}}(c^{\prime})
.

By Theorem
21
we have

\mathord{!}n;\mathsf{do}\ {gcs}\ \mathsf{od}\>\mathrel{\simeq}\>\mbox{{add}}^{pc}(c);\mathord{!}f\qquad\mbox{and}\qquad\mathord{!}n^{\prime};\mathsf{do}\ {gcs^{\prime}}\ \mathsf{od}\>\mathrel{\simeq}\>\mbox{{add}}^{pc}(c^{\prime});\mathord{!}f^{\prime}

Define store relation
\mathcal{Q}
to be
\mathcal{Q}_{an}\land\mathcal{Q}_{pc}
where

\begin{array}[]{l}\mathcal{Q}_{an}:\qquad(\land\,i,j\>:\>i\in\mbox{{labs}}(c)\cup\{f\}\land j\in\mbox{{labs}}(c^{\prime})\cup\{f^{\prime}\}\>:\>\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.1pt[$}\mathord{?}i\mathbin{\mid}\mathord{?}j\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$]\hskip-2.1pt\rangle$ }\Rightarrow an(i,j))\\
\mathcal{Q}_{pc}:\qquad(\lor\,i,j\>:\>i\in\mbox{{labs}}(c)\cup\{f\}\land j\in\mbox{{labs}}(c^{\prime})\cup\{f^{\prime}\}\>:\>\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.1pt[$}\mathord{?}i\mathbin{\mid}\mathord{?}j\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$]\hskip-2.1pt\rangle$ })\end{array}

We will derive

\mathsf{do}\ {gcs}\ \mathsf{od}\mathbin{\mid}\mathsf{do}\ {gcs^{\prime}}\ \mathsf{od}:\mathcal{Q}\mathrel{\mbox{\footnotesize$\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\exists}}{{\raisebox{-0.24113pt}{$\thickapprox$}\hskip-4.13332pt>}}$}}\mathcal{Q}\land\mbox{$\lnot$}\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.0pt[$}\mbox{{enab}}(gcs)\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.1pt]$}\land\mbox{$\lnot$}\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$[\hskip-2.1pt\rangle$}\mbox{{enab}}(gcs^{\prime})\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$]\hskip-2.0pt\rangle$}

(33)

To do so, we start by noting
K
being an adequate filtering implies there
is a variant function
V
from

((\mbox{{labs}}(c)\cup\{f\})\times(\mbox{{labs}}(c^{\prime})\cup\{f^{\prime}\}))\times((\mbox{{Var}}\to\mathbb{Z})\times(\mbox{{Var}}\to\mathbb{Z}))

to
D
, where
(D,\prec)
is some well-ordered set. Define the

pc
-encoded variant

\tilde{V}
as

\tilde{V}(s,s^{\prime})\mathrel{\,\hat{=}\,}V((s(pc),s^{\prime}(pc)),(s,s^{\prime}))
. Note that
\tilde{V}
is a function
on variable stores. In what follows, we will also use the curried form

{V}^{\mbox{\tiny$\downarrow$}}(n,n^{\prime})
defined for any
n,n^{\prime},s,s^{\prime}
by

{V}^{\mbox{\tiny$\downarrow$}}(n,n^{\prime})(s,s^{\prime})\mathrel{\,\hat{=}\,}V((n,n^{\prime}),(s,s^{\prime}))
.

Now to show (
33
), we instantiate rule
eDo
with
\mathcal{Q}:=\mathcal{Q}
,

\mathcal{L}:=\tilde{L}
,
\mathcal{R}:=\tilde{R}
and
V:=\tilde{V}
. The side
condition of
eDo
is the same as the side condition of
rDo
.
Further, the instantiation of
\mathcal{Q}
,
\mathcal{L}
, and
\mathcal{R}
is the same as in the
proof of Theorem
25
. Thus, the proof that the side
condition is valid is identical. We now turn to proofs of the premises of

eDo
. For each, we list a few representative cases.

There are three sets of premises of
eDo
for the two loops, with these
forms:

(left-only)

b\mathbin{\mid}\mathsf{skip}:\mathcal{Q}\land\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.0pt[$}e\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.1pt]$}\land\tilde{L}\mathrel{\mbox{\footnotesize$\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\exists}}{{\raisebox{-0.24113pt}{$\thickapprox$}\hskip-4.13332pt>}}$}}\mathcal{Q}

for all
e\mathrel{\shortrightarrow}b
in
gcs

(right-only)

\mathsf{skip}\mathbin{\mid}b^{\prime}:\mathcal{Q}\land\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$[\hskip-2.1pt\rangle$}e^{\prime}\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$]\hskip-2.0pt\rangle$}\land\tilde{R}\land(\tilde{V}=k)\mathrel{\mbox{\footnotesize$\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\exists}}{{\raisebox{-0.24113pt}{$\thickapprox$}\hskip-4.13332pt>}}$}}\mathcal{Q}\land(\tilde{V}\prec k)
for all
e^{\prime}\mathrel{\shortrightarrow}b^{\prime}
in
gcs^{\prime}
,
all
k\in D

(joint)

b\mathbin{\mid}b^{\prime}:\mathcal{Q}\land\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.1pt[$}e\mathbin{\mid}e^{\prime}\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$]\hskip-2.1pt\rangle$ }\land\mbox{$\lnot$}\tilde{L}\land\mbox{$\lnot$}\tilde{R}\mathrel{\mbox{\footnotesize$\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\exists}}{{\raisebox{-0.24113pt}{$\thickapprox$}\hskip-4.13332pt>}}$}}\mathcal{Q}
for all
e\mathrel{\shortrightarrow}b
in
gcs
and
e^{\prime}\mathrel{\shortrightarrow}b^{\prime}
in
gcs^{\prime}

Joint cases

We start by making note of the following.
For any
m
and
m^{\prime}
we can prove

\mathord{!}m\mathbin{\mid}\mathord{!}m^{\prime}:an(m,m^{\prime})\mathrel{\mbox{\footnotesize$\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\exists}}{{\raisebox{-0.24113pt}{$\thickapprox$}\hskip-4.13332pt>}}$}}\mathcal{Q}.

(34)

We arrive at this as follows. Since
an(m,m^{\prime})
is independent of
pc
on
both sides, the judgment

\mathord{!}m\mathbin{\mid}\mathord{!}m^{\prime}:an(m,m^{\prime})\mathrel{\mbox{\footnotesize$\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\exists}}{{\raisebox{-0.24113pt}{$\thickapprox$}\hskip-4.13332pt>}}$}}an(m,m^{\prime})\land\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.1pt[$}\mathord{?}m\mathbin{\mid}\mathord{?}m^{\prime}\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$]\hskip-2.1pt\rangle$ }

can be proved using
eAsgnAsgn
with
eConseq
to simplify the precondition.
Then, we use
eConseq
with the fact that

an(m,m^{\prime})\land\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.1pt[$}\mathord{?}m\mathbin{\mid}\mathord{?}m^{\prime}\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$]\hskip-2.1pt\rangle$ }\Rightarrow\mathcal{Q}
to
obtain (
34
).
Additionally, the following implication is valid.

\mathcal{Q}\land\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.1pt[$}\mathord{?}n\mathbin{\mid}\mathord{?}n^{\prime}\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$]\hskip-2.1pt\rangle$ }\land\mbox{$\lnot$}\tilde{L}\land\mbox{$\lnot$}\tilde{R}\Rightarrow an(n,n^{\prime})\land\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.1pt[$}\mathord{?}n\mathbin{\mid}\mathord{?}n^{\prime}\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$]\hskip-2.1pt\rangle$ }\land\tilde{J}

(35)

We arrive at this using adequacy condition (c) of the theorem
(in particular, the enabled condition in Def.
9.1
)
and the fact that
Q\land\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.1pt[$}\mathord{?}n\mathbin{\mid}\mathord{?}n^{\prime}\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$]\hskip-2.1pt\rangle$ }\Rightarrow an(n,n^{\prime})
.
See (
22
).

Now for the joint premises. We spell out just two cases.

•

\mathsf{hav}\ {x};\;\mathord{!}m\mathbin{\mid}\mathsf{skip};\;\mathord{!}m^{\prime}:\mathcal{Q}\land\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.1pt[$}\mathord{?}n\mathbin{\mid}\mathord{?}n^{\prime}\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$]\hskip-2.1pt\rangle$ }\land\mbox{$\lnot$}\tilde{L}\land\mbox{$\lnot$}\tilde{R}\mathrel{\mbox{\footnotesize$\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\exists}}{{\raisebox{-0.24113pt}{$\thickapprox$}\hskip-4.13332pt>}}$}}\mathcal{Q}
,
where
\mbox{{sub}}(n,c)=\mathsf{hav}^{n}\ {x}
,
m=\mbox{{fsuc}}(n,c,f)
,

\mbox{{sub}}(n^{\prime},c^{\prime})=\mathsf{skip}^{n^{\prime}}
, and
m^{\prime}=\mbox{{fsuc}}(n^{\prime},c^{\prime},f^{\prime})
.

We construct a deductive proof using
eSeq
with judgments (
34
)
and

\mathsf{hav}^{n}\ {x}\mathbin{\mid}\mathsf{skip}^{n^{\prime}}:\mathcal{Q}\land\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.1pt[$}\mathord{?}n\mathbin{\mid}\mathord{?}n^{\prime}\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$]\hskip-2.1pt\rangle$ }\land\mbox{$\lnot$}\tilde{L}\land\mbox{$\lnot$}\tilde{R}\mathrel{\mbox{\footnotesize$\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\exists}}{{\raisebox{-0.24113pt}{$\thickapprox$}\hskip-4.13332pt>}}$}}an(m,m^{\prime})

To prove the latter, first by
eHavSkip
we have
\mathsf{hav}^{n}\ {x}\mathbin{\mid}\mathsf{skip}^{n^{\prime}}:(\dot{\forall}x\mbox{\tiny$|$}.\>an(m,m^{\prime}))\mathrel{\mbox{\footnotesize$\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\exists}}{{\raisebox{-0.24113pt}{$\thickapprox$}\hskip-4.13332pt>}}$}}an(m,m^{\prime})
. Now strengthen the precondition using
eConseq

for which we need to show
\mathcal{Q}\land\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.1pt[$}\mathord{?}n\mathbin{\mid}\mathord{?}n^{\prime}\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$]\hskip-2.1pt\rangle$ }\land\mbox{$\lnot$}\tilde{L}\land\mbox{$\lnot$}\tilde{R}\Rightarrow(\dot{\forall}x\mbox{\tiny$|$}.\>an(m,m^{\prime}))
. In doing so, we’ll
appeal to the corresponding
pc
-encoded VC (Lemma
32
) which is

\forall v\in\mathbb{Z}.\>an(n,n^{\prime})\land\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.1pt[$}\mathord{?}n\mathbin{\mid}\mathord{?}n^{\prime}\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$]\hskip-2.1pt\rangle$ }\land\tilde{J}\land{{K}^{\mbox{\tiny$\downarrow$}}(m,m^{\prime})}^{x|}_{v|}\Rightarrow{an(m,m^{\prime})}^{x|}_{v|}

The
eConseq
step is justified as follows.

\begin{array}[]{lll}&\mathcal{Q}\land\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.1pt[$}\mathord{?}n\mathbin{\mid}\mathord{?}n^{\prime}\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$]\hskip-2.1pt\rangle$ }\land\mbox{$\lnot$}\tilde{L}\land\mbox{$\lnot$}\tilde{R}\\
\Rightarrow&an(n,n^{\prime})\land\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.1pt[$}\mathord{?}n\mathbin{\mid}\mathord{?}n^{\prime}\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$]\hskip-2.1pt\rangle$ }\land\tilde{J}&\mbox{by~(\ref{eqn:aejoint})}\\
\Rightarrow&an(n,n^{\prime})\land\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.1pt[$}\mathord{?}n\mathbin{\mid}\mathord{?}n^{\prime}\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$]\hskip-2.1pt\rangle$ }\land\tilde{J}\land(\forall v\in\mathbb{Z}.\>{{K}^{\mbox{\tiny$\downarrow$}}(m,m^{\prime})}^{x|}_{v|})&\mbox{semantics, adequacy, see below $(\dagger)$}\\
\Leftrightarrow&\forall v.\>an(n,n^{\prime})\land\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.1pt[$}\mathord{?}n\mathbin{\mid}\mathord{?}n^{\prime}\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$]\hskip-2.1pt\rangle$ }\land\tilde{J}\land{{K}^{\mbox{\tiny$\downarrow$}}(m,m^{\prime})}^{x|}_{v|}&\mbox{pred calc}\\
\Rightarrow&\forall v.\>{an(m,m^{\prime})}^{x|}_{v|}&\mbox{apply VC (Lemma~\ref{lem:liftEVC})}\\
\Leftrightarrow&\dot{\forall}x\mbox{\tiny$|$}.\>an(m,m^{\prime})&\mbox{by def}\end{array}

To justify the step marked
(\dagger)
above, we
rely on the theorem’s assumption of adequate filtering.
Specifically, we use use joint-productivity to prove that

an(n,n^{\prime})\land\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.1pt[$}\mathord{?}n\mathbin{\mid}\mathord{?}n^{\prime}\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$]\hskip-2.1pt\rangle$ }\land\tilde{J}

implies
(\forall v\in\mathbb{Z}.\>{{K}^{\mbox{\tiny$\downarrow$}}(m,m^{\prime})}^{x|}_{v|})
.
Because Def.
9.1
is in terms of automata states,
we need to reason pointwise and take care with our notational abuses.
Observe for any
s,s^{\prime}
that

(s,s^{\prime})\in(an(n,n^{\prime})\land\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.1pt[$}\mathord{?}n\mathbin{\mid}\mathord{?}n^{\prime}\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$]\hskip-2.1pt\rangle$ }\land\tilde{J})

equivales, by definitions and freshness of
pc
,

(s,s^{\prime})\in an(n,n^{\prime})\land s(pc)=n\land s^{\prime}(pc)=n^{\prime}\land((n,n^{\prime}),(s,s^{\prime}))\in J

By the assumptions
\mbox{{sub}}(n,c)=\mathsf{hav}^{n}\ {x}
and
m=\mbox{{fsuc}}(n,c,f)
,
using
subsection 4.3
of program automata
which in this case is based on semantics of
\mathsf{hav}^{n}\ {x}
,

we have
(n,s)\mapsto(m,s[x\mbox{$\,\mapsto\,$}v])
for all
v\in\mathbb{Z}
.
By joint-productivity, for any
v
there is some
m^{\prime\prime},t^{\prime}

such that
(n^{\prime},s^{\prime})\mapsto^{\prime}(m^{\prime\prime},t^{\prime})
and
((m,m^{\prime\prime}),(s[x\mbox{$\,\mapsto\,$}v]))\in K
.
But by semantics and assumptions
\mbox{{sub}}(n^{\prime},c^{\prime})=\mathsf{skip}^{n^{\prime}}
and
m^{\prime}=\mbox{{fsuc}}(n^{\prime},c^{\prime},f^{\prime})

we must have
m^{\prime\prime}=m^{\prime}
and
t^{\prime}=s^{\prime}
, so we have

((m,m^{\prime}),(s[x\mbox{$\,\mapsto\,$}v],s^{\prime}))\in K
.
This is equivalent to

(s[x\mbox{$\,\mapsto\,$}v],s^{\prime})\in{K}^{\mbox{\tiny$\downarrow$}}(m,m^{\prime})

and to

(s,s^{\prime})\in{{K}^{\mbox{\tiny$\downarrow$}}(m,m^{\prime})}^{x|}_{v|}
.
So we have

(\forall v\in\mathbb{Z}.\>{{K}^{\mbox{\tiny$\downarrow$}}(m,m^{\prime})}^{x|}_{v|})
,
so
(\dagger)
is proved.

•

\mathord{!}m\mathbin{\mid}\mathord{!}m^{\prime}:\mathcal{Q}\land\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.1pt[$}\mathord{?}n\mathbin{\mid}\mathord{?}n^{\prime}\land e^{\prime}\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$]\hskip-2.1pt\rangle$ }\land\mbox{$\lnot$}\tilde{L}\land\mbox{$\lnot$}\tilde{R}\mathrel{\mbox{\footnotesize$\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\exists}}{{\raisebox{-0.24113pt}{$\thickapprox$}\hskip-4.13332pt>}}$}}\mathcal{Q}

where

\mbox{{sub}}(n,c)=\mathsf{skip}^{n}
,
m=\mbox{{fsuc}}(n,c,f)
,

\mbox{{sub}}(n^{\prime},c^{\prime})=\mathsf{if}^{n^{\prime}}\ {gcs^{\prime}_{0}}\ \mathsf{fi}
with
e^{\prime}\mathrel{\shortrightarrow}d^{\prime}
in
gcs^{\prime}_{0}
,
m^{\prime}=\mbox{{lab}}(d^{\prime})
.

The corresponding VC is similar to the assign/if VC in
Figure 19
,
specifically:

J\land\breve{an}(n,n^{\prime})\land\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$[\hskip-2.1pt\rangle$}e^{\prime}\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$]\hskip-2.0pt\rangle$}\land K(m,m^{\prime})\Rightarrow\hat{an}(m,m^{\prime})

Of course we use the pc-encoded version.

We prove the judgment using (
34
) and
eConseq
with the following implication.

\begin{array}[]{lll}&\mathcal{Q}\land\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.1pt[$}\mathord{?}n\mathbin{\mid}\mathord{?}n^{\prime}\land e^{\prime}\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$]\hskip-2.1pt\rangle$ }\land\mbox{$\lnot$}\tilde{L}\land\mbox{$\lnot$}\tilde{R}\\
\Rightarrow&an(n,n^{\prime})\land\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.1pt[$}\mathord{?}n\mathbin{\mid}\mathord{?}n^{\prime}\land e^{\prime}\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$]\hskip-2.1pt\rangle$ }\land\tilde{J}&\mbox{using (\ref{eqn:aejoint})}\\
\Rightarrow&an(n,n^{\prime})\land\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.1pt[$}\mathord{?}n\mathbin{\mid}\mathord{?}n^{\prime}\land e^{\prime}\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$]\hskip-2.1pt\rangle$ }\land\tilde{J}\land{K}^{\mbox{\tiny$\downarrow$}}(m,m^{\prime})&\mbox{adequacy, semantics}\\
\Rightarrow&an(m,m^{\prime})&\mbox{apply VC}\end{array}

The adequacy step uses in particular joint productivity, which says for any
n,n^{\prime},s,s^{\prime},m,t

that if
(s,s^{\prime})\in an(n,n^{\prime})
and
((n,n^{\prime}),(s,s^{\prime}))\in J
and
(n,s)\mapsto(m,t)
, then
there are
m^{\prime\prime},t^{\prime}
with
(n^{\prime},s^{\prime})\mapsto^{\prime}(m^{\prime\prime},t^{\prime})
and
((m,m^{\prime\prime}),(t,t^{\prime}))\in K
.
Instantiating this with
m:=\mbox{{fsuc}}(n,c,f)
and
t:=s
(since the command at
n
is skip),
we get that there exist
m^{\prime\prime},t^{\prime}

with
(n^{\prime},s^{\prime})\mapsto^{\prime}(m^{\prime\prime},t^{\prime})
and
((m,m^{\prime\prime}),(t,t^{\prime}))\in K
.
By semantics and control determinacy (assumption (a) of the theorem,
and Lemma
33
) and the fact that
e^{\prime}
is true in
s^{\prime}
,
we have that
m^{\prime\prime}=m^{\prime}
since
m^{\prime}
is the unique successor,
and by semantics
t^{\prime}=s^{\prime}
.
So we have

(n^{\prime},s^{\prime})\mapsto^{\prime}(m^{\prime},s^{\prime})
and
((m,m^{\prime}),(s,s^{\prime}))\in K
,
and equivalently
(s,s^{\prime})\in{K}^{\mbox{\tiny$\downarrow$}}(m,m^{\prime})
.

Right-only cases

These cases are proved using the same rules as the joint cases, plus one
additional rule:
eDisj
, in the form
eDisjN
derived from it. This
is needed for the same reason
rDisjN
is needed in the proof of
alignment completeness for RHL+ (
25
). The joint cases
determine a unique starting and ending pair of control points, which
determines the VC to appeal to. The right-only cases do not determine a
control point on the left.
So we consider an arbitrary left control point,
and then combine all cases using
eDisjN
.
In passing, we note the following:

(s,s^{\prime})\in\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.1pt[$}\mathord{?}m\mathbin{\mid}\mathord{?}m^{\prime}\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$]\hskip-2.1pt\rangle$ }\Rightarrow{V}^{\mbox{\tiny$\downarrow$}}(m,m^{\prime})(s,s^{\prime})=\tilde{V}(s,s^{\prime})\quad\mbox{for any $s,s^{\prime},m,m^{\prime}$}

(36)

\mathcal{Q}\land\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$[\hskip-2.1pt\rangle$}\mathord{?}n^{\prime}\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$]\hskip-2.0pt\rangle$}\Leftrightarrow(\lor\,n\>:\>n\in\mbox{{labs}}(c)\cup\{f\}\>:\>\mathcal{Q}\land\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.1pt[$}\mathord{?}n\mathbin{\mid}\mathord{?}n^{\prime}\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$]\hskip-2.1pt\rangle$ })

(37)

Additionally, to streamline the proofs below, we note the following can be proved.

\mathsf{skip}\mathbin{\mid}\mathord{!}m^{\prime}:an(n,m^{\prime})\land\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.0pt[$}\mathord{?}n\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.1pt]$}\land{V}^{\mbox{\tiny$\downarrow$}}(n,m^{\prime})\prec k\mathrel{\mbox{\footnotesize$\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\exists}}{{\raisebox{-0.24113pt}{$\thickapprox$}\hskip-4.13332pt>}}$}}\mathcal{Q}\land\tilde{V}\prec k

(38)

We obtain this judgment as follows. By
eSkipAsgn
and the fact that all
the terms in the precondition are independent of
pc
on the right, we have

\mathsf{skip}\mathbin{\mid}\mathord{!}m^{\prime}:an(n,m^{\prime})\land\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.0pt[$}\mathord{?}n\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.1pt]$}\land{V}^{\mbox{\tiny$\downarrow$}}(n,m^{\prime})\prec k\mathrel{\mbox{\footnotesize$\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\exists}}{{\raisebox{-0.24113pt}{$\thickapprox$}\hskip-4.13332pt>}}$}}an(n,m^{\prime})\land\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.1pt[$}\mathord{?}n\mathbin{\mid}\mathord{?}m^{\prime}\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$]\hskip-2.1pt\rangle$ }\land{V}^{\mbox{\tiny$\downarrow$}}(n,m^{\prime})\prec k
. We then weaken the postcondition using

eConseq
: suppose

an(n,m^{\prime})\land\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.1pt[$}\mathord{?}n\mathbin{\mid}\mathord{?}m^{\prime}\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$]\hskip-2.1pt\rangle$ }\land{V}^{\mbox{\tiny$\downarrow$}}(n,m^{\prime})\prec k
.
By (
21
), this implies

\mathcal{Q}\land\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.1pt[$}\mathord{?}n\mathbin{\mid}\mathord{?}m^{\prime}\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$]\hskip-2.1pt\rangle$ }\land{V}^{\mbox{\tiny$\downarrow$}}(n,m^{\prime})\prec k
.
We then have
\mathcal{Q}\land\tilde{V}\prec k
by (
36
).

We now detail the proofs for a couple of representative right-only cases. Towards that end, we
pick an arbitrary control point
n\in\mbox{{labs}}(c)\cup\{f\}
on the left.
The judgments below conjoin
\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.0pt[$}\mathord{?}n\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.1pt]$}
to preconditions, and hence, don’t
exactly match the premises of
eDo
. However, since
n
is an arbitrary label,
by (
37
) and application of
eDisjN
, each proof below
gives rise to a corresponding right-only premise of rule
eDo

(such use of a disjunction rule is spelled out in more detail in the proof of

25
).
Note there’s a premiss for each literal value
k
in
D
.
So let
k\in D
be arbitrary, in the following.

•

\mathsf{skip}\mathbin{\mid}\mathord{!}m^{\prime}:\mathcal{Q}\land\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.1pt[$}\mathord{?}n\mathbin{\mid}\mathord{?}n^{\prime}\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$]\hskip-2.1pt\rangle$ }\land\tilde{R}\land\tilde{V}=k\mathrel{\mbox{\footnotesize$\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\exists}}{{\raisebox{-0.24113pt}{$\thickapprox$}\hskip-4.13332pt>}}$}}\mathcal{Q}\land\tilde{V}\prec k
, where

\mbox{{sub}}(n^{\prime},c^{\prime})=\mathsf{skip}^{n^{\prime}}
and
m^{\prime}=\mbox{{fsuc}}(n^{\prime},c^{\prime},f^{\prime})
.

We obtain this from (
38
) by
eConseq
, strengthening the precondition as follows.

\begin{array}[]{lll}&\mathcal{Q}\land\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.1pt[$}\mathord{?}n\mathbin{\mid}\mathord{?}n^{\prime}\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$]\hskip-2.1pt\rangle$ }\land\tilde{R}\land\tilde{V}=k\\
\Rightarrow&an(n,n^{\prime})\land\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.1pt[$}\mathord{?}n\mathbin{\mid}\mathord{?}n^{\prime}\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$]\hskip-2.1pt\rangle$ }\land\tilde{R}\land{V}^{\mbox{\tiny$\downarrow$}}(n,n^{\prime})=k&\mbox{(\ref{eq:IanRel}) and~(\ref{eqn:VCright})}\\
\Rightarrow&an(n,n^{\prime})\land\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.1pt[$}\mathord{?}n\mathbin{\mid}\mathord{?}n^{\prime}\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$]\hskip-2.1pt\rangle$ }\land\tilde{R}\land{K}^{\mbox{\tiny$\downarrow$}}(n,m^{\prime})\land{V}^{\mbox{\tiny$\downarrow$}}(n,m^{\prime})\prec k&\mbox{adequacy, semantics $(\dagger)$}\\
\Rightarrow&an(n,m^{\prime})\land\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.0pt[$}\mathord{?}n\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.1pt]$}\land{V}^{\mbox{\tiny$\downarrow$}}(n,m^{\prime})\prec k&\mbox{apply VC (Lemma~\ref{lem:liftEVC})}\end{array}

In the step marked
(\dagger)
, we use the semantics of
\mathsf{skip}

and apply the right-productivity condition,
noting that the only transitions from
n^{\prime}
are to
m^{\prime}
,
to obtain
{K}^{\mbox{\tiny$\downarrow$}}(n,m^{\prime})\land{V}^{\mbox{\tiny$\downarrow$}}(n,m^{\prime})\prec{V}^{\mbox{\tiny$\downarrow$}}(n,n^{\prime})
.
Using
{V}^{\mbox{\tiny$\downarrow$}}(n,n^{\prime})=k
,
this yields
{K}^{\mbox{\tiny$\downarrow$}}(n,m^{\prime})\land{V}^{\mbox{\tiny$\downarrow$}}(n,m^{\prime})\prec k
.
As in the proofs of the joint-only
premises of
eDo
above, we do not spell this step out in detail.

•

\mathsf{skip}\mathbin{\mid}\mathord{!}m^{\prime}:\mathcal{Q}\land\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.1pt[$}\mathord{?}n\mathbin{\mid}\mathord{?}n^{\prime}\land e^{\prime}\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$]\hskip-2.1pt\rangle$ }\land\tilde{R}\land\tilde{V}=k\mathrel{\mbox{\footnotesize$\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\exists}}{{\raisebox{-0.24113pt}{$\thickapprox$}\hskip-4.13332pt>}}$}}\mathcal{Q}\land\tilde{V}\prec k
, where

\mbox{{sub}}(n^{\prime},c^{\prime})=\mathsf{if}^{n^{\prime}}\ {gcs^{\prime}}\ \mathsf{fi}
and
e^{\prime}\mathrel{\shortrightarrow}d^{\prime}
is in
gcs^{\prime}
and

m^{\prime}=\mbox{{lab}}(d^{\prime})
.

As in the preceding case we obtain this from (
38
) by
eConseq
, strengthening the precondition as follows.

\begin{array}[]{lll}&\mathcal{Q}\land\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.1pt[$}\mathord{?}n\mathbin{\mid}\mathord{?}n^{\prime}\land e^{\prime}\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$]\hskip-2.1pt\rangle$ }\land\tilde{R}\land\tilde{V}=k\\
\Rightarrow&{\qquad\mbox{ (\ref{eq:IanRel}) and~(\ref{eqn:VCright})}}\\
&an(n,n^{\prime})\land\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.1pt[$}\mathord{?}n\mathbin{\mid}\mathord{?}n^{\prime}\land e^{\prime}\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$]\hskip-2.1pt\rangle$ }\land\tilde{R}\land{V}^{\mbox{\tiny$\downarrow$}}(n,n^{\prime})=k\\
\Rightarrow&{\qquad\mbox{$(\dagger)$ adequacy, semantics, see below}}\\
&an(n,n^{\prime})\land\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.1pt[$}\mathord{?}n\mathbin{\mid}\mathord{?}n^{\prime}\land e^{\prime}\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$]\hskip-2.1pt\rangle$ }\land\tilde{R}\land{K}^{\mbox{\tiny$\downarrow$}}(n,m^{\prime})\land{V}^{\mbox{\tiny$\downarrow$}}(n,m^{\prime})\prec k\\
\Rightarrow&{\qquad\mbox{apply VC from \autoref{fig:EVCro}, see \autoref{lem:liftEVC}}}\\
&an(n,m^{\prime})\land\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.0pt[$}\mathord{?}n\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.1pt]$}\land{V}^{\mbox{\tiny$\downarrow$}}(n,m^{\prime})\prec k\end{array}

To prove the step marked
(\dagger)
, consider any
(s,s^{\prime})
that satisfies the antecedent.
By right productivity there are
m^{\prime\prime},t^{\prime}
such that

(n^{\prime},s^{\prime})\mapsto^{\prime}(m^{\prime\prime},t^{\prime})
and
((n,m^{\prime\prime}),(s,t^{\prime}))\in K
and
V((n,m^{\prime\prime}),(s,t^{\prime}))\prec V((n,n^{\prime}),(s,t^{\prime}))
.
By definition of automata and semantics of if, the store is unchanged so
t^{\prime}
is
s^{\prime}
.
That is, we have
m^{\prime\prime}
such that
(n^{\prime},s^{\prime})\mapsto^{\prime}(m^{\prime\prime},s^{\prime})
and
((n,m^{\prime\prime}),(s,s^{\prime}))\in K

and
V((n,m^{\prime\prime}),(s,s^{\prime}))\prec V((n,n^{\prime}),(s,s^{\prime}))
.
Because
(s,s^{\prime})
satisfies
\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$\langle\hskip-2.1pt[$}\mathord{?}n\mathbin{\mid}\mathord{?}n^{\prime}\land e^{\prime}\raisebox{1.07639pt}{$]\hskip-2.1pt\rangle$ }
and
c^{\prime}
is control deterministic, we must have
m^{\prime\prime}=m^{\prime}
, hence

((n,m^{\prime}),(s,s^{\prime}))\in K
and
V((n,m^{\prime}),(s,s^{\prime}))\prec V((n,n^{\prime}),(s,s^{\prime}))
.
So
(s,s^{\prime})
satisfies
{K}^{\mbox{\tiny$\downarrow$}}(n,m^{\prime})
and
{V}^{\mbox{\tiny$\downarrow$}}(n,m^{\prime})\prec k

as needed for the consequent of
(\dagger)
.

The other right-only cases are similarly proved.

Left-only cases

These are very similar to the right-only cases and are omitted.
Justifications of
eConseq
are simpler since we don’t have to reason
about
{V}^{\mbox{\tiny$\downarrow$}}
decreasing. Rather than using right-productivity of
K
, these cases use its left-permissivity.

Finishing the proof

Now that we’ve established all the premises of
eDo
, the rest of the
proof proceeds exactly like the proof of
25

but using
eSeq
,
eConseq
,
eRewrite
,
eGhost
.
